About:

### iWallet: Autonomous, Intent-Centric DeFi Infrastructure

### **Elevator Pitch**

iWallet is an autonomous, intent-driven smart wallet infrastructure built on Sui. It allows users to execute complex, multi-step DeFi strategies using natural language, entirely automating repetitive tasks while eliminating the need to expose long-lived private keys to AI agents. It is secure, intent-driven, and fully verifiable on-chain.

### **The Problem: The Friction of Decentralized Finance**

As finance moves toward AI-driven execution, the current wallet architecture is fundamentally broken. We identified three critical bottlenecks preventing mass adoption of Agentic DeFi:

- **The Agentic Security Flaw (Key Custody):** Handing long-lived private keys over to autonomous AI agents creates massive security vulnerabilities and single points of failure. There is currently no safe infrastructure for AI to transact on behalf of a user without compromising the master key.

- **The Execution Barrier (UX Friction):** Participating in advanced DeFi (like DeepBook's central limit order books) requires deep technical knowledge, an understanding of slippage, pool IDs, and manual transaction signing. This steep learning curve actively gatekeeps average users from participating in the best yield and trading opportunities.

- **The Attention Tax (Manual Repetition):** DeFi is exhausting. Executing basic liquidity strategies, claiming yields, rebalancing portfolios, and making recurring payments require constant manual intervention. Users are forced to act as manual operators rather than high-level financial strategists.

### **The Solution: iWallet**

iWallet abstracts the complexities of blockchain interactions by acting as an intelligent translation layer between human intent and on-chain execution.

Instead of navigating rigid frontends, users interact with iWallet via natural language. The system autonomously translates these commands into mathematically precise Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs) and executes them securely using delegated capability models.

### **Core Features**

- **Ephemeral & Delegated Execution:** We eliminate long-lived private keys for agentic finance. iWallet utilizes Sui's object-centric capabilities (such as DeepBook's `TradeCap`) and scoped permissions. This allows the AI to execute trades and manage liquidity without ever possessing the user's master key.

- **Natural Language to On-Chain Action:** Users simply state their financial intent in plain English (e.g., *"Send 10 SUI to Ayoola, then use the rest to place a limit order for USDC at market price"*). iWallet autonomously parses, routes, and compiles the request into a composable PTB.

- **Autonomous Automation:** iWallet handles repetitive chores. It is designed to monitor, trade, and execute multi-step financial workflows in the background based on the user's predefined parameters.

- **Trustless Auditability:** Every AI-generated decision, routing parameter, and automated workflow is recorded transparently on-chain, ensuring verifiable and immutable proof of execution.

### **Technical Architecture (Under the Hood)**

iWallet is powered by a high-performance backend (Hono/TypeScript) paired with a specialized Multi-Agent LLM routing system. Currently built as a lightning-fast CLI application utilizing the Nautilus framework, the architecture is broken down into distinct, composable intelligence layers:

- **The Orchestrator (xAI Grok):** Acts as the central nervous system. It ingests the user's natural language prompt and breaks it down into a sequential, actionable execution plan, ensuring composability across multiple DeFi actions.

- **DeepBook V3 Trader (Claude 3.5 Sonnet):** A specialized worker agent equipped with dynamic RAG lookup tools. It queries active Mainnet/Testnet pool configurations in real-time, calculates slippage, and constructs highly complex DeepBook limit/market order PTBs.

- **Wallet Operations (Moonshot Kimi):** Handles standard DeFi utility actions natively, translating intent into standard object transfers, staking operations, and fast swaps.

- **The Policy Guardian (Qwen):** A deterministic safety layer ensuring that no AI-generated transaction exceeds predefined spend limits, slippage thresholds, or security boundaries before compilation.

### **Why Sui?**

Sui's unique Object-Centric architecture is the only environment where iWallet's vision is fully possible today. By utilizing **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs)**, iWallet can stitch together outputs from multiple different AI agents (e.g., a transfer from Kimi + a limit order from Claude) into a single, atomic, gas-efficient transaction. Furthermore, Sui's capability objects allow us to solve the AI private key dilemma natively at the protocol level.

About:

**Built by 4 UGM BCC (Universitas Gadjah Mada Blockchain Club) Staff**

Dien Muhammad Scientivan K. as Technical Departemen Lead

Diaz Amantajati S. as Media Co-Lead of Marketing Departemen

Galang Swastika R. as Frontend Lead of Technical Departemen

Raditya Azhar A. as Backend Lead of Technical Departemen

**The Problem :**

Every LP tool today analyzes positions one by one. But what if all your positions bleed for the same reason? A user with 5 LP positions across Cetus, Turbos, and DeepBook might think they're diversified, but 87% of their value is secretly correlated to ETH. When ETH drops 10%, they lose ~$1,800 across ALL positions at once. No existing tool catches this.

**How Luber Works :**

Luber is a chat-first AI agent with three modes:

1. "Am I safe?" Scans all LP positions on-chain (Sui RPC + Pyth feeds), then responds in plain English: "You think you have 5 positions. You actually have 1 bet, 87% ETH."

2. "Guard it." One signature mints a revocable StrategistCap. The agent can rebalance within whitelisted pools but physically cannot withdraw funds, enforced by Move's type system.

3. Autonomous defense. Price shock hits, agent acts with zero clicks: detects the cluster bleeding, rebalances via PTB on-chain, reports how much it saved with a transaction link.

**Non-Custodial Security**

Positions live in a Portfolio shared object governed by a revocable StrategistCap. The Move module only allows rebalance (whitelisted pools + bounded slippage) and withdraw (always to owner). No function transfers assets to an arbitrary address. Fund exfiltration is structurally impossible.

**What Makes Luber Unique**

- Portfolio-level correlation. No other Sui tool detects that "5 positions" are secretly one correlated bet.

- Transparent counterfactual. "Money saved" uses a visible formula, never a fabricated number.

- MCP-native. First Sui LP agent accessible through any MCP-compatible AI host, not just a web dashboard.

- Explainable. Every action has a human-readable reason and on-chain proof via immutable HealthReport objects.

**Tech Stack**

- React + Vite + @mysten/dapp-kit | Node.js + @mysten/sui + @mysten/deepbook-v3 | Python FastAPI (NumPy/SciPy) |

- Move on Sui Testnet | Pyth oracles | MCP SDK

**Track**

Agentic Web: AI agents acting autonomously on-chain with Sui-native capability-based security

**Why Sui?**

- Object composition. Portfolio to Position parent-child ownership mirrors real-world portfolio structure.

- Capability objects. StrategistCap enables revocable, type-safe, epoch-expiring agent permissions.

- PTB. Atomic multi-step rebalancing in a single transaction.

- Shared objects. Agent acts autonomously without owner co-signing every rebalance.

- Immutable objects. HealthReport freezes analysis results as tamper-proof audit trail.

About:

Sentinel is an autonomous risk-control layer for Sui DeFi lending markets. It watches market risk in real time, scores risk from 0-100 using a deterministic engine, and when thresholds are crossed, executes bounded on-chain safety actions on Sui Testnet such as reducing max LTV, pausing new borrows, or entering guarded mode.

The core safety idea is that the AI never controls the protocol. The agent only holds a Move GuardianCap scoped to specific markets, actions, deltas, cooldowns, and expiry. It cannot move funds, change admins, or raise its own limits. The DAO holds an OverrideCap and can reverse, retune, unpause, or revoke the agent.

Sentinel uses live Pyth prices, DeepBook liquidity, real Suilend reserve anchoring, Walrus evidence storage, and Sui Move capability enforcement. Every autonomous action writes evidence to Walrus and links it to an on-chain action log, making decisions auditable and governable.

About:

Pinace is the first wallet that lets AI agents trade for users without ever holding their keys.

Other agent wallets hand the AI your private key — Pinace hands it a budget and a rulebook. The owner funds an on-chain pool, attaches Move-enforced policies (per-tx cap, per-window cap, slippage guard), and any wallet-standard dApp can drive an agent inside those bounds. One signed transaction kills it forever.

Every action is one atomic transaction on Sui — propose, prove the policy, swap, settle. Break a rule, the whole transaction reverts. No EVM allowance sweep, no per-token revoke race.

Any agent platform — chat bots, MCP servers, custom dApps — plugs into Pinace through our **@pinace/core TypeScript SDK** in two Wallet Standard calls. Alongside it we shipped a Chrome MV3 wallet extension, an indexer, and our agent poc Fenik — our reference chat agent that quotes DeepBook, checks the user's policy, and ships the swap in one signature.

Built on Sui Move + DeepBook v3, signed via Wallet Standard.

**
What we shipped:**

- Wallet extension — Chrome MV3, owner-controlled, Slush-quality UX with approval popup + Stop button during agent execution: [https://github.com/pinace-wallet/frontend](https://github.com/pinace-wallet/frontend)

- Move contracts live on Sui testnet (balance_pool, delegation, policy modules): [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0x5be5ab0292590e61e2dc21d11cc7174970f48e5ff54c88d4797c81b2a751a23b](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0x5be5ab0292590e61e2dc21d11cc7174970f48e5ff54c88d4797c81b2a751a23b)

- @pinace/core SDK on npm — PTB builders, policies, read client: [https://www.npmjs.com/package/@pinace/core](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@pinace/core)

- Fenik — reference agent POC, conversational DeepBook trading, live at [https://fenik.one](https://fenik.one) · [https://github.com/pinace-wallet/pinace-agent](https://github.com/pinace-wallet/pinace-agent)

About:

Orca Sentinel is a non-custodial security layer for AI agents that handle money on

  Sui.

  It protects agentic finance by putting funds inside a user-owned Move vault and

  giving the agent only limited, policy-bound authority. The owner controls the

  vault, while the agent can only act within deterministic on-chain rules like

  spending caps, allowlisted recipients, expiry, revocation, swap limits, and an

  emergency pause.

  If an agent is prompt-injected, compromised, or goes rogue, Orca Sentinel does not

  rely on the AI model to catch the attack. The Move vault enforces the rules on-

  chain, with zero AI in the allow/block path.

  Orca also includes a console for monitoring live vault state, reviewing policy,

  running sandboxed attack simulations against a clone of the user’s config, checking

  security posture, verifying Walrus evidence, and using Ask Orca, a read-only AI

  copilot that explains the system without holding keys or moving funds.

  In short: Orca Sentinel gives money-holding AI agents deterministic guardrails,

  real attack testing, and verifiable proof.

  Tweet: [https://x.com/jester_keri/status/2068852764789064026?s=20](https://x.com/jester_keri/status/2068852764789064026?s=20)

About:

Coral is an autonomous agent wallet on Sui that lets AI agents execute on-chain actions within strict, enforceable constraints defined on-chain. It enables agents to trade on DeepBook and run strategies without per-action approvals, while remaining strictly bounded by a capped budget, protocol/token scope, and time expiry.

This matters because it removes the “approval bottleneck” that prevents AI agents from acting in real time, while ensuring safety through verifiable on-chain policy enforcement, activity logs, and instant owner revocation if needed.

About:

**SuiFlow - Visual On-Chain Automation for Sui**

Build the workflow once. Let Sui run it.

SuiFlow is a node-graph automation engine for Sui. Instead of writing a bespoke bot for every on-chain action, you wire Trigger, Logic, and Action nodes on a visual canvas - the engine watches the chain and signs real Programmable Transaction Blocks on your behalf, paying gas in SUI.

**The Problem**

On-chain activity on Sui is manual and reactive. A balance crossing a threshold, an object's fields changing, a stake becoming withdrawable - none of that triggers anything by itself. Acting on it today means writing a script, hosting it, and handing that script a private key. Most users never get past step one.

**What We Built**

**Visual workflow builder **- a node canvas where triggers, conditions, and chain actions are wired together. Branch ports (true/false, switch cases), stop/filter gating, and {{NodeTitle.output.field}} references pass data between nodes.

**Real chain execution **- workflows run against Sui testnet. Multi-step actions compile to a single atomic Programmable Transaction Block - all-or-nothing, no partial-failure state.

**Dry-run vs live-run** - /test evaluates the full graph with reads and gas estimates via devInspectTransactionBlock, submits nothing. /run signs and submits with the user's keypair.

**Per-user custodial wallet** - a Sui Ed25519 keypair is generated on signup. The mnemonic is Fernet-encrypted at rest and never exposed. Every resource is owner_id-scoped.

**41 executor nodes** across 7 categories.

- **Triggers: **manual, schedule.

- **Logic: **condition, filter, switch, set_var, math, merge, collect, foreach, stop, wait.

- **Chain reads: **get_object, get_balance, get_owned_objects, get_coin_metadata, get_dynamic_field, get_staking.

- **Chain writes: **sui_transfer, ptb_execute, move_call, ptb_transfer.

- **Walrus: **walrus_store (returns blob ID and a [walruscan.com](http://walruscan.com) explorer link), walrus_read.

- **AI: **call_llm, ai_gate (LLM-as-condition), ai_summarize, ai_nft_meta. Notify: telegram, discord, slack, http_notify, http_fetch.

- **TypeScript SDK **- @suiflow/sdk, a zero-dependency typed REST client and workflow-as-code builder whose per-node methods are codegen'd from the live executor catalog.

**Demo video:** [https://youtu.be/1pxbj6QFhno](https://youtu.be/1pxbj6QFhno)

**Technical Highlights**

**PTBs as the unit of automation** - multi-step workflows compile to one atomic transaction instead of one-per-node, so execution either fully lands or fully fails.

**Thread-safe async RPC** - every chain op runs in [asyncio. to](http://asyncio.to)_thread and is serialized behind a process-wide threading.RLock so the async API never races the blocking pysui client.

**Graph interpreter **- BFS from the trigger over graph.edges, respecting branch ports, stop/filter gating, and {{ref}} resolution against a per-run output map. Each run is persisted as a runs document plus ordered run_steps for full replay.

**Single source of truth** - the node catalog is defined once in the frontend, generated into the backend, and filtered at GET /node-types to executor-backed types. The UI structurally cannot offer a node the backend can't run.

**Tracks Covered**

**Agentic Web** - AI nodes inline (call_llm, ai_gate, ai_summarize) plus the SDK lets agents author, dry-run, and execute on-chain automations via function calls.

**Infra and DevX **- BFS execution engine, 41-node typed catalog, codegen'd SDK, extensible by adding new executor nodes.

**DeFi **- Real SUI transfers, PTB execution, and Move calls against Sui testnet, with [testnet.suivision.xyz](http://testnet.suivision.xyz) explorer links.

**Walrus Storage** - walrus_store and walrus_read nodes store any output to Walrus and return a blob ID plus a [walruscan.com](http://walruscan.com) explorer URL as referenceable outputs.

**Stack**

**Frontend: **Next.js 14, React 18, node-graph canvas.

**Backend:** FastAPI, Motor (async MongoDB), Pydantic v2.

**Chain: **pysui, handling Ed25519 signing, PTB construction, and RPC.

**Auth: **JWT (HS256), bcrypt, Fernet for wallet encryption.

**AI: **OpenAI and Anthropic, with provider-aware dispatch. SDK: TypeScript, built with tsup (ESM and CJS), codegen'd from the catalog.

Links

GitHub: [https://github.com/anbusan19/suiflow](https://github.com/anbusan19/suiflow)

SDK: npm install @suiflow/sdk

Pitch deck: [https://suiflow-hub.vercel.app/suiflow-pitch.html](https://suiflow-hub.vercel.app/suiflow-pitch.html)

About:

**Glyph** is a transaction risk explainer for Sui agents.

Paste a testnet digest and Glyph decodes the PTB, runs** 19 deterministic risk rules** (slippage, unknown packages, transfer size, pool gates), renders a mermaid command graph, and optionally records the verdict on-chain via `policy::record_verdict`.

No LLM in the verdict path: every classification is reproducible from public chain data.

**Features**

- Deterministic digest analyzer. Paste any testnet digest; Glyph fetches `sui_getTransactionBlock`, decodes every PTB command, and runs nineteen risk rules with zero LLM in the scoring path. Same digest always yields the same verdict; judges re-run the CLI and match the web card. Say Ur Intent compiles forward; we explain history. Proof: Chorus DeepBook digest `8BryXGJof6k44oRMi9jGafFBGe5F5CkA5NzNoTY2qFdb` returns identical severity in web and `npx tsx src/cli.ts`.

- Mermaid PTB preview. The analyzer expands a command graph: MoveCall, TransferObjects, SplitCoins nodes colored by risk tier. DeepBook `place_limit_order` and fleet `execute_deepbook_order` calls highlight before any wallet signs. Competitors show intent diagrams; we render the actual executed PTB from RPC. Proof: expand mermaid on the `8BryXGJo` scan and count commands against Suiscan.

- BLOCK / ALLOW / REVIEW verdict. Rules aggregate into an overall severity with named flag classes (CONCENTRATION, UNKNOWN_PACKAGE, SLIPPAGE, pool gates). Demo chip drain (BLOCK) on digest `4CoY9EpcuKMs8BsDG18expvWZ8DaWsdnmxwvs9nSSBb5` shows CRITICAL shape Tako policy wallets should reject. Proof: BLOCK demo digest and badge URL `?badge=8BryXGJo…&network=testnet`.

- On-chain `record_verdict`. Optional wallet path calls `record_verdict_checked` on the Glyph Policy object; verdict hash keyed by digest closes the audit loop. Third parties verify the on-chain receipt without trusting our frontend. Proof: verdict recorded tx `8VMrNH9rTwaHhZc7nxNnpv3iZAXzhv4NRNGAp5sPf5HD`, policy package `0x7cac1e39ecc7c2c615c9831cdcb1b4d39683b95b59a2977ff270d7039bdcf857`.

- Intent tab: compile then assess. Natural language compiles to a real Transaction; deterministic guardian plus dryRun simulation runs before Confirm and execute unlocks. Small transfer intent to a known address returns ALLOW with Mermaid preview. OpenRouter LLM is optional; offline parser fallback is labelled in UI. Proof: intent input `send 0.5 SUI to 0x4dbdd19fbc0fb81382f57a684e2554626a42cb099dd1a531666d90b4e9fc2d15`.

- Guardrail `assess()` pre-sign hook. The guardrail tab builds raw PTB bytes locally and calls `assess()` in sub-millisecond time with zero RPC for static rules. Drain Attempt returns BLOCKED with CONCENTRATION flags; Sweep Drain uses `combinedAssess()` where simulation catches net balance drain static rules miss. Agent integrators import `@glyph/sui-guard` for the same path. Proof: BLOCKED panel on Drain Attempt demo in guardrail tab.

- Policy studio from wallet history. Analyze history scans last fifty txs and recommends max qty, daily cap, slippage, and allowed pools. Create policy on-chain emits a BalanceManager plus SpendPolicy PTB composable with Tako. Glyph is the reverse reviewer

**On-chain proof**

|----------|-------------|

About:

***[Video Link in **[*README.md*](http://README.md)**]***

- **Business Pain Points**: Enterprise adoption of stablecoins and DeFi is surging, but financial teams struggle with fragmented data formats across exchanges/custodians, mismatching accounting policies (IFRS vs US GAAP), and manual reconciliation close processes. Additionally, traditional subledgers cannot parse Sui's object-centric transaction schemas.
.

- **Solution**: TallyMarina standardises Sui transaction blocks and object states into unified financial events, uses a safe AI assistant (without posting authority) to recommend account classifications, and anchors period-close Merkle roots on the Sui blockchain.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Sui-Native Normalisation**: Converts complex object states into unified financial ledger events.

- **AI-Assisted Classification**: Recommends categories and metadata with human-in-the-loop review.

- **Double-Entry Journal Engine**: Translates events into balanced debit/credit ledger entries.

- **Tamper-Evident Auditing (**`AuditAnchor`**)**: Cryptographically chains close-period hashes on the Sui blockchain.

About:

Neura is a decentralized artifact layer for AI agents built on Sui.

Instead of losing expert corrections inside chats, Neura transforms them into reusable knowledge artifacts that can be installed, shared, and used to improve agent performance across specialized domains.

In this demo, we showcase artifact-powered reasoning, compare raw LLM responses against artifact-enhanced responses, and demonstrate how Sui and Walrus enable ownership, provenance, and decentralized storage for AI knowledge.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026.

About:

***[Video Link in **[*README.md*](http://README.md)**]***

- **Business Pain Points**: DeFi user experiences are notoriously complex. Users face opaque transaction signatures (raw hex or unparsed Move structures), leading to blind-signing (nearly 95% of users blind-sign, causing millions in exploit losses). Furthermore, relying solely on LLMs to generate transactions is highly dangerous due to potential hallucinations.
.

- **Solution**: LexiFlow compiles natural-language commands (e.g. "move my idle USDC from Scallop to Navi") into secure, atomic Sui PTBs. It simulates proposed transactions in a local sandbox to inspect them against strict safety policies and presents the user with a plain-English risk card prior to zkLogin signature execution.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Natural-Language to PTB Compiler**: Converts user statements into multi-protocol atomic transaction blocks.

- **Deterministic Guardian Layer**: Inspects PTBs for stale oracle feeds (>60s), thin liquidity pools, and fee anomalies.

- **zkLogin Integration**: Eliminates seed phrase friction via Google/Apple OAuth onboarding.

- **White-Label SDK**: Enables Web2 platforms to integrate secure stablecoin and yield settlement with a single line of code.

About:

### ***SeFi + Liqify***

### ***Building Autonomous Risk Guardians for the Agentic Web on Sui***

Built on SeFi, Liqify converts blockchain activity into semantic risk intelligence and autonomously protects lending positions before liquidation occurs.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026 — Agentic Web / Autonomous Risk Guardian Track

### **The Bigger Problem**

The future of the Agentic Web is not limited by AI models.

It is limited by data.

Blockchains generate enormous amounts of information every second. Lending protocols, DEXs, oracle networks, and smart contracts continuously emit events, update objects, and create new state transitions.

While all of this data is publicly available, it is extremely difficult for AI agents to understand and reason about directly.

Each protocol uses its own:

- Data structures

- Events

- Objects

- State models

- Risk metrics

As a result, developers repeatedly build custom indexers, parsers, and analytics systems just to answer basic questions such as:

- Which positions are close to liquidation?

- Which markets are becoming risky?

- Which users require intervention?

- What actions should an agent take?

- Is the data fresh enough to trust?

Raw blockchain data exists everywhere.

Semantic intelligence does not.

Without a common understanding layer, autonomous agents cannot safely operate across decentralized finance.

### **Introducing SeFi**

### Semantic Finance Infrastructure

SeFi is a protocol-agnostic semantic intelligence layer for Sui.

Its mission is simple:

Transform raw blockchain activity into structured financial knowledge that applications and AI agents can understand.

Instead of forcing developers and agents to reason about low-level blockchain events, SeFi exposes high-level concepts that are meaningful and actionable.

Examples include:

- Borrow Positions

- Health Factors

- Reserve Coverage

- Market Conditions

- Liquidity Scores

- Liquidation Exposure

- Risk Indicators

- Portfolio Risk

- Oracle Freshness

SeFi acts as the intelligence layer between blockchain data and autonomous applications.

### **What SeFi Indexes**

SeFi continuously indexes and processes data from across the Sui ecosystem.

Current sources include:

- Scallop lending markets

- NAVI lending markets

- DeepBook liquidity and order-book depth

- Oracle price feeds

- Sui checkpoints

- Protocol state transitions

- On-chain governance activity

Instead of exposing protocol-specific formats, SeFi converts everything into a unified semantic model.

Applications can consume a single representation of risk regardless of which protocol generated the original data.

### **Protocol-Agnostic by Design**

SeFi is designed to support the broader Sui ecosystem.

Each protocol is integrated through protocol manifests that define:

- Package IDs

- Event Types

- Object Reads

- Parsing Rules

- Risk Mapping Logic

Adding support for a new protocol does not require rebuilding the indexing infrastructure.

Developers simply create a new manifest and parser.

This allows SeFi to scale across lending, trading, derivatives, stablecoins, and future DeFi primitives.

### **Cube-Powered Intelligence Layer**

Above SeFi sits Cube.

Cube transforms indexed data into governed semantic metrics that AI systems can safely consume.

Examples include:

- Distance to Liquidation

- Reserve Coverage Ratio

- Oracle Freshness

- Liquidity Score

- Market Stress Index

- Volatility Indicators

- Rescue Feasibility Metrics

Applications never need to query raw blockchain events directly.

Instead, they interact with structured concepts backed by transparent computations.

This significantly reduces hallucination risk while improving reliability and auditability.

### **Continuous Learning Loop**

Every application built on SeFi contributes additional intelligence back into the ecosystem.

Applications emit structured events that SeFi re-indexes and analyzes.

Examples include:

- Risk updates

- Automated actions

- Position changes

- Rescue outcomes

- Policy decisions

This creates a feedback loop where autonomous systems can continuously learn from historical outcomes while maintaining complete transparency.

### **Why SeFi Matters**

SeFi enables a new generation of applications on Sui.

Applications no longer need to build custom data infrastructure.

AI agents no longer need to interpret raw blockchain state.

Developers can focus on building intelligent products rather than indexing pipelines.

SeFi becomes the shared intelligence layer powering autonomous financial applications.

### **Introducing Liqify**

### The First Autonomous Risk Guardian Built on SeFi

Liqify is a Sui-native autonomous pre-liquidation safety system powered by SeFi.

It serves as a practical demonstration of how semantic intelligence can enable autonomous financial protection.

While SeFi provides structured risk intelligence, Liqify converts that intelligence into real-world protective actions.

### **The Problem**

Every day, DeFi users lose positions not because they made poor decisions, but because they were not online at the right moment.

On lending protocols such as Scallop and NAVI, borrowers become vulnerable when:

- Collateral prices fall

- Volatility increases

- Liquidity deteriorates

- Market conditions shift rapidly

Once a position crosses the liquidation threshold, liquidation bots immediately step in, repay debt, and seize collateral for profit.

Current tools are reactive.

They send alerts after risk increases and expect users to manually respond.

But risk does not wait for users to wake up, check notifications, or reconnect to the internet.

### **The Liqify Solution**

Liqify continuously monitors borrowing positions using SeFi's semantic risk intelligence.

Instead of reacting after liquidation occurs, it intervenes before liquidation happens.

When risk reaches a critical threshold, Liqify automatically executes a bounded rescue using a pre-funded emergency reserve.

Possible rescue actions include:

- Partial debt repayment

- Collateral top-up

- Position risk reduction

- Policy-approved protective actions

No custodial access.

No unlimited spending permissions.

No AI-controlled treasury.

Every action remains constrained by on-chain policies enforced through Move smart contracts.

The result is an autonomous safety layer for DeFi borrowing.

### **How Liqify Uses SeFi**

SeFi continuously computes:

- Health Factors

- Distance to Liquidation

- Oracle Freshness

- Liquidity Conditions

- Market Stress Levels

- Reserve Coverage

Liqify consumes these semantic metrics and transforms them into autonomous decisions.

Rather than reasoning about raw blockchain events, the agent operates using verified risk concepts.

This creates a safer and more reliable decision-making process.

### **System Architecture**

Liqify operates through four coordinated layers.

### **1. Data Plane (SeFi + Cube)**

The Data Plane transforms blockchain activity into structured risk intelligence.

Responsibilities:

- Protocol indexing

- Oracle monitoring

- DeepBook liquidity analysis

- Semantic risk computation

- Governed metric exposure

This layer serves as the source of truth for downstream decisions.

### **2. Agent Plane**

The autonomous risk agent evaluates positions and determines whether intervention is necessary.

Risk score inputs include:

- Position Risk (45%)

- Oracle Risk (20%)

- Liquidity Risk (15%)

- Market Volatility (10%)

- Reserve Inadequacy (10%)

The agent generates:

- Risk explanations

- Rescue recommendations

- Rescue simulations

- Confidence scores

### **3. Move Enforcement Plane**

Move smart contracts enforce strict operational boundaries.

Controls include:

- Spending limits

- Protocol allowlists

- Capability-based permissions

- Rescue budgets

- Expiration windows

- Owner revocation controls

- DAO override mechanisms

Even if an AI model behaves unexpectedly, it cannot bypass these constraints.

### **4. Execution Plane**

Approved rescue actions execute through a single atomic Sui Programmable Transaction Block (PTB).

Execution flow:

- Validate policy

- Verify capability

- Withdraw emergency reserve

- Execute debt repayment

- Emit audit events

- Update position state

If any step fails, the entire transaction reverts.

### **Why Sui?**

Liqify leverages capabilities that are uniquely powerful on Sui.

These include:

- Object-centric asset ownership

- Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs)

- Capability-based security

- Atomic multi-step execution

- High-throughput low-latency performance

These primitives allow complex rescue operations to execute safely and atomically without introducing custodial risk.

### **Risk Bands**

### Normal (0–44)

- Observe position status

- Log health metrics

- No user action required

### Watch (45–69)

- Increase monitoring frequency

- Surface dashboard warnings

- Track changing market conditions

### Guarded (70–84)

- Generate rescue strategy

- Simulate rescue PTB

- Notify user of elevated risk

### Emergency (85–100)

- Execute bounded rescue

- Verify policy compliance

- Validate oracle freshness

- Require successful simulation

### Paused / Revoked

- Disable autonomous actions

- Display revocation reason

- Preserve audit trail

### **Safety First**

Liqify follows a strict fail-closed philosophy.

If any safety check fails, no action is executed.

Examples include:

- Stale oracle data

- Failed transaction simulation

- Expired authorization

- Budget exhaustion

- Revoked permissions

- Invalid protocol state

The system always prefers inaction over unsafe execution.

### **Demo Flow**

The demonstration showcases a complete autonomous rescue lifecycle.

- A Scallop borrowing position begins in a healthy state.

- Market conditions deteriorate.

- The risk score rises from Normal to Emergency.

- SeFi detects increasing liquidation exposure.

- Liqify generates a rescue strategy.

- Rescue actions are simulated.

- A rescue PTB executes automatically.

- A ShieldActivatedEvent is emitted on-chain.

- The position returns to a safer state.

- A DAO or owner override prevents additional rescue attempts.

The entire process remains transparent, auditable, and verifiable on-chain.

### **Vision**

SeFi creates the semantic intelligence layer for autonomous finance on Sui.

Liqify demonstrates what becomes possible when AI agents can reason using structured financial knowledge rather than raw blockchain data.

Today, Liqify protects lending positions.

Tomorrow, SeFi can power:

- Autonomous treasury management

- Cross-protocol risk management

- AI portfolio guardians

- Automated yield protection

- Institutional monitoring systems

- Agentic financial infrastructure

**Together, SeFi and Liqify introduce a new category for decentralized finance:**

### **Autonomous Risk Guardians for the Agentic Web.**

About:

Radiant isn’t a chatbot, a DeFi app, or a no-code builder — it’s all of that in one personal agent that acts, remembers, builds, trades, and earns on your behalf.

Talk to it in plain language. It researches, automates, and moves on-chain when you need to. When a task repeats, Radiant builds you a personal app — private tools for yourself, or fan apps and mini apps you can share on Radiant. Every app exposes an API, so other users and other agents can call it. List what you build in the explorer, set a fee, and earn. Radiant is your agent — and the start of an agent economy.

**What it does for you**

- **Researches** — searches the internet, reads webpages, looks up docs and news, compares options, and answers questions with up-to-date information. It can also query onchain data — balances, pool info, market activity — so research isn’t limited to what it already knows.

- **Acts** — swaps, payments, protocol interactions, reminders, and automated workflows. You say what you want; Radiant executes.

- **Remembers** — wallets, apps, credentials, preferences, and history across every session. You don’t repeat yourself.

- **Builds** — personal apps from a single sentence: portfolio trackers, automations, fan apps, mini apps for your community.

- **Trades** — onchain execution when you need it — swaps, orders, deposits, and more — without navigating a dozen interfaces.

- **Earns** — list what you build in the Radiant explorer; humans and agents use it, and fees flow to you.

**What you can build**

- **Personal apps** — portfolio trackers, automations, tools only you use

- **Fan apps** — community tools, branded experiences, shared utilities

- **Mini apps** — lightweight apps other Radiant users can discover and run

- **Agent-callable APIs** — every app is composable; other AI agents can invoke yours as a building block

**The bigger picture**

- An **agent economy**: humans build mini apps → agents orchestrate them → creators earn → the ecosystem compounds

About:

MeshAction is a multi-agent execution and verification network built on Sui.

As AI agents become capable of researching information, calling external tools, coordinating with other agents, and executing transactions, a new challenge emerges: how can these actions be trusted?

MeshAction provides a workflow where agent actions are proposed, inspected, verified, executed, and audited. Instead of treating agents as black boxes, MeshAction makes every step visible before execution and verifiable afterward.

Users can connect a Sui wallet, bring their own agents, and coordinate multi-agent workflows through a unified execution console. Agents can collaborate, gather information, prepare transactions, and perform real on-chain actions while remaining subject to policy checks, user review, and cryptographically verifiable receipts.

Built on Sui, MeshAction leverages programmable transactions, wallet signatures, Walrus, Seal, and the SuiMesh network to provide secure execution, encrypted archives, and trace restoration.

MeshAction is an application built on top of SuiMesh.

While SuiMesh focuses on communication and coordination between humans and agents, MeshAction focuses on trusted execution. Together, they provide the foundation for a future where multiple AI agents can safely collaborate across wallets, applications, protocols, and real-world workflows.

Our vision is a world where specialized agents work together to research, decide, and act. MeshAction ensures those actions remain transparent, accountable, and verifiable.

About:

### *Make any Sui Protocols usable by AI agents.*

**The thesis — Software for Agents**

*"The next wave of internet users will be AI agents, not humans."* — [Y Combinator RFS, Software for Agents](https://www.ycombinator.com/rfs#software-for-agents)

-

YC's RFS makes the case bluntly: today's software was built for humans clicking through UIs — for agents it's *"slow, inconsistent, and brittle."* Agents need **machine-readable interfaces (APIs, MCPs, CLIs)** and enough context to **discover and use tools programmatically, without a human in the loop.** *"Every major category of software needs to be rebuilt for agents"* — by startups that treat agents as **first-class citizens**, not by incumbents bolting on agent support. **Rill is that rebuild for the most consequential category on Sui: moving money.**

-

On-chain, the human-era assumptions are everywhere, wallets that pop up to click "approve," SDKs written for developers to read, ABIs that mean nothing without docs. An agent hits a wall:

-

- - it **can't read intent** — an ABI shows arg0, arg1, arg2, so the agent hallucinates and builds the wrong transaction;

- - it **can't get past "approve"** — every action wants a human signature, or the agent holds a raw key that can drain everything;

- - it **can't act safely on its own** — no budget, no scope, no slippage floor, no kill switch.

**Rill** rebuilds that layer for agents: **machine-readable** (one flow, exposed as MCP / REST / Skill — exactly the interfaces the RFS names), **self-describing** (semantic parameters, so agents stop guessing), and **safe to run unattended** (keyless build + two on-chain chokepoints that bound autonomy). It is the software agents depend on to transact on Sui — *"Something Agents Want,"* built agent-first.

About:

**Altheia**

A non-custodial control plane for AI agents on Sui.

An autonomous agent that moves money needs bounds the chain enforces, not bounds you hope it respects. altheia gives each agent its own key and a vault governed by an on-chain Move Policy object: per-transaction and per-day spend caps, an allowlist of protocols and actions, and an expiry. The contract checks every signature. An over-budget or out-of-scope action reverts on-chain. The owner revokes in one transaction, and funds return to their wallet. Every action, allowed or denied, is recorded in an audit trail keyed to the agent.

**Live on Sui testnet**

- **Move policy objects** — capped budget, protocol scope, expiry, pause, revoke, enforced in `policy::check_and_consume`

- **DeepBook adapter** — policy-gated swaps

- `@altheia-xyz/sui` SDK** — an agent author writes about five lines; the SDK derives the agent's vault and policy from its key and builds the gated transaction

- **Operator dashboard** — provision an agent, read holdings and policy live from chain, revoke and withdraw

- **Audit chronicle** — every gated action, on-chain

**Repos**

- Contracts (Move): `github.com/altheia-xyz/altheia-sui`

- Demo agent: `github.com/altheia-xyz/altheia-sui-demo`

- SDK: `npmjs.com/package/@altheia-xyz/sui`

Sui-native today. The same control plane runs on Solana.

About:

***[Video Link in **[*README.md*](http://README.md)**]***

- **Business Pain Points**: DeFi lending and perpetual protocols managing billions in TVL rely on static risk parameters (LTV, borrow caps, interest rates) and slow DAO governance processes. When stablecoins de-peg or oracle prices deviate, markets collapse in seconds, causing millions in bad debt before human governors can react.
.

- **Solution**: TideWard bridges off-chain risk intelligence and on-chain execution. It runs an autonomous risk scoring agent that calculates risk indices in real time and automatically dispatches atomic Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs) to adjust parameters on a Sui Move `RiskPolicy` object, whilst preserving a first-class DAO override capability to rollback adjustments.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Autonomous On-Chain Enforcement**: Executes parameter adjustments such as LTV capping and market pausing.

- **Move Policy Objects**: VM-level assertion gates checking policy states prior to lending executions.

- **First-Class DAO Override**: Single-click rollbacks via the `OverrideCap` held by the DAO multisig.

- **Granular Blast Radius**: Policy scoping per pool or market rather than protocol-wide globals.

About:

**HexLiquid Yield**

A non-custodial, TEE-attested autonomous DeFi treasury agent, live on Sui mainnet.

A non-custodial multi-agent that deploys idle stablecoins under on-chain risk bounds you can cryptographically verify. The LLM plans(One Main Agent + 6 Independent SubAgents), a decision layer runs strategies inside TEE and explains; a deterministic layer moves funds. Shared Walrus State and Memories

**Elevator pitch**

Letting an AI agent manage real funds usually means trusting two things you can't verify: the operator who runs it and the LLM that decides. HexLiquid Yield removes both. Your funds stay in an on-chain Treasury, the agent only proposes, a secure enclave (TEE) signs the actual allocation, and a Move contract verifies that signature and your caps on-chain before any coin moves. Owner-only withdrawal. An autonomous yield agent you don't have to trust.

**The problem**

Autonomous DeFi agents today are custodial black boxes. They hold your keys and you hope they behave. As soon as an LLM can move money, you inherit two unverifiable trust assumptions: the host operator, and the model's decisions.

**What we built**

HexLiquid Yield is a non-custodial treasury agent where every fund movement is bounded and cryptographically verifiable:

1. **Decide:** An LLM agent and a deterministic six-subagent yield-loop pipeline propose an allocation, both bounded by the same policy.

2. **Attest:** The optimizer runs and signs the decision inside a TEE (Nautilus + Marlin Oyster). Its key is generated in the enclave and bound on-chain by PCRs, so the agent can't puppet the venue or amount, and a tampered intent is rejected.

3. **Verify:** The Move contract checks the enclave signature, the adapter allow-list, the replay nonce, and the per-tx and rolling caps, on-chain, before releasing a single coin.

4. **Custody:** Funds deploy across Suilend, NAVI, and Scallop; the protocol receipt stays inside the non-custodial Treasury (owner-only withdrawal), with an auditable history on Walrus.

**Live on Sui mainnet**

The full loop runs live across all three major Sui money markets. The `treasury_core` package, the attested `Enclave<DECISION>`, and per-protocol adapter packages (Scallop, NAVI, Suilend) are all deployed on mainnet, with roughly 130 tests across Move, the agent, and the enclave.

**Architecture**

A split on-chain design: a protocol-free `treasury_core` (the Treasury, scoped revocable capabilities, the enclave-signature verifier, the seal policy) plus one adapter package per protocol, so no package ever sees two protocols' conflicting dependencies. `verified_release` hands out a bounded coin together with a `ReleaseTicket` hot-potato that can only be discharged by custodying the protocol receipt inside the vault, so funds can't escape even across package boundaries. NAVI, which is address-bound, stays non-custodial via its `AccountCap` held by the Treasury.

**Tech stack**

- **Sui:** Move 2024, `@mysten/sui`, `@mysten/dapp-kit`, native `nitro_attestation`

- **TEE and attestation:** Nautilus (Sui's verifiable-compute framework) on Marlin Oyster (AWS Nitro enclaves); `@noble` crypto inside the enclave for a small, reproducible PCR

**- Custom Built Typescript multi agent framework with 6 subagent pipeline**

- **DeFi:** Suilend, NAVI, Scallop (on-chain adapters + SDKs), Pyth price feeds

- **Storage and memory:** **Walrus** (verifiable agent state + run reports), **MemWal** (semantic memory), Seal (sealed key, scaffolded)

- **Frontend:** a read-only Trust Console (Next.js) that surfaces live rates, positions, strategy proposals, execution receipts, and the agent's reasoning

## Why it matters

Autonomous agents will manage real money. HexLiquid shows how to do it without asking users to trust the operator or the model: non-custodial by construction, with the decision itself attested in hardware and verified on-chain.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/88452daa-cff1-4108-a70d-d7c5b33df703/d39317c1-63d9-481f-a932-b7f750a7377f.png

About:

AI agents can already perform paid work, but there’s no reliable way to verify whether their output is correct or to hold them accountable when it isn’t—reputation today often boils down to something as weak as a screenshot. Vouch introduces a trust layer where agent reliability becomes provable, insured, and even tradable.

When you hire a bonded agent, your payment is placed in escrow rather than paid upfront. The agent completes the task—whether it’s auditing a Move package, analyzing a coin, optimizing a swap route, or reporting on a wallet—and an independent auditor then re-derives the ground truth directly from on-chain data. For more subjective tasks, a stronger, separate model evaluates the result. Payment is only released if the output matches the verified result.

Every job produces an evidence bundle containing the input, output, and verdict, which is stored on Walrus. Settlement is atomic: if the agent passes, they are paid; if they fail, you receive a refund, the agent earns nothing, and their staked bond is slashed. Over time, each agent’s reliability is quantified as the YES mid-price in a DeepBook v3 pool, continuously re-anchored to its on-chain performance after every job. This creates a live, tradable reputation market for agents.

The system distinguishes between objectively provable tasks—verified directly on-chain—and open-ended tasks, which are judged on a best-effort basis and clearly labeled as such rather than “proven.” Currently live on Sui testnet, the system includes Move contracts for agent registries, slashable bonds, parametric insurance, and atomic resolution, along with curated agents, real DeepBook markets, Walrus-based evidence storage, and a revenue model combining a 10% take rate with underwriting margins. Analysis pulls from Sui mainnet, while payments are settled on testnet as project is currently is on testnet.

About:

Vektor is an intent engine for Sui that  and puts Defi at your fingertips ,It turns plain language into a quoted, risk-scored, ready-to-sign transaction , and understands 20+ languages natively, no translation step.. Before anything signs,Vektor's Guardian checks it against 7 risk classes: price impact, slippage, stale quotes, thin liquidity, gas sufficiency, protocol concentration, and trade size. A high-risk flag blocks execution outright, not just warnings.

Vektor doesn't stop at proposing it acts autonomously. Key features include scheduled payments and swaps, conditional exits that fire on price triggers, DCA and rebalancing on a cadence, batch and split payments, and seedless onboarding via zkLogin that gets new users transacting in seconds without owning SUI for gas. Autonomous, limit-bounded execution comes through Echo.

Built on Sui's PTBs for atomic multi-step execution, zkLogin for seedless onboarding, Walrus for encrypted session-key storage, and SuiNS for human-readable addressing. 22 intent types ,swap, lend, borrow, repay, DCA, conditional exits, rebalancing, scheduled and batch payments , routed across Deepbook , aftermath and cetus DEXes via our own published SDK, Routex.

About:

# SuiVault

SuiVault is an AI-powered decentralized vault built on Sui that enables users to securely store, organize, and retrieve digital assets through natural language interactions.

## Problem

Managing important files, credentials, notes, and digital assets across multiple platforms is inefficient, insecure, and difficult to scale. Traditional cloud solutions provide storage but lack ownership, transparency, and intelligent retrieval.

## Solution

SuiVault combines Sui's high-performance infrastructure with AI-powered semantic search to create a secure, user-owned digital vault. Users can upload assets, automatically categorize content, and instantly retrieve information using natural language queries.

## Key Features

- Secure decentralized asset storage

- AI-powered semantic search

- Natural language retrieval

- Smart categorization and indexing

- User-controlled ownership and permissions

- Fast and scalable architecture on Sui

## Why SuiVault?

Unlike traditional storage platforms, SuiVault combines blockchain ownership, intelligent search, and decentralized infrastructure to create a next-generation personal knowledge and asset management system.

## Impact

SuiVault empowers individuals, creators, students, professionals, and organizations to securely manage their digital assets while maintaining full ownership and benefiting from AI-assisted productivity.

About:

Jetpack lets autonomous agents transact with each other using programmable payment capabilities instead of raw wallet access. Each capability is a Sui object encoding a spending policy, max amount, allowed counterparties, time window, enforced on-chain at the object level. Because capabilities are independent objects, agents can pay each other concurrently without contending for a shared ledger entry. Built to show what agent-to-agent payments look like at machine speed on Sui.

About:

**DeepSense** is a personal AI risk advisor for Sui DeFi. It connects to a user's wallet, scans positions across Navi, Scallop, Cetus, and DeepBook, and gives a single risk score explained in plain English instead of raw health factors.

When the AI recommends an action, like adding collateral to avoid liquidation, it shows a preview first: protocol, amount, before/after risk impact, and flagged risk warnings. Nothing executes until the user confirms. Only then does it compile into a Sui Programmable Transaction Block and go to their wallet for signing.

This intent-to-execution flow is built specifically for the Agentic Web track's Intent Engine sub-track at Sui Overflow 2026.

About:

SuiPass lets you give AI agents a spending card instead of your private key.

Like a corporate credit card for AI, users can set a budget, expiry date, and merchant restrictions, then provide the card URL to any AI agent through MCP. The agent can spend USDC within those limits on Sui. Users can revoke access instantly at any time. No seed phrases and no key sharing are required.

TEAM & PROJECT INFORMATION

Project Name: SuiPass

Track: Agentic Web

Sub-track: Autonomous Agent Wallet

Repository:
[https://github.com/LSUDOKO/SuiPass](https://github.com/LSUDOKO/SuiPass)

Dashboard:
[https://dashboard-blond-phi-67.vercel.app](https://dashboard-blond-phi-67.vercel.app)

Server:
[https://suipass-server.onrender.com](https://suipass-server.onrender.com)

Health Check:
[https://suipass-server.onrender.com/health](https://suipass-server.onrender.com/health)

Network:
Sui Testnet

Move Package:
0x1d020a948ce614e47c60d3fa36b90a90e74672878fc881f3091272735a14e969

PROBLEM STATEMENT

AI agents such as Claude Code, ChatGPT, Cursor, and Gemini CLI are becoming autonomous digital workers, but they currently lack a safe way to spend money on behalf of users.

Current approaches have major security limitations:

- Sharing a private key gives the agent complete control of the wallet.

- Creating separate wallets provides no budget controls or revocation mechanism.

- Pre-funded API wallets rely on centralized trust and lack on-chain verification.

- Server-managed approvals are not transparent and require trust in a central authority.

WHY THIS MATTERS

AI agents are increasingly responsible for purchasing APIs, compute resources, subscriptions, and other services. Without secure spending controls, businesses cannot safely delegate financial authority to agents.

SuiPass solves this problem by turning every spending card into a first-class Sui object with on-chain policy enforcement.

SOLUTION

SuiPass is a self-hosted card issuance platform where:

• Users sign in using Google through zkLogin.
• Users create spending cards with programmable limits.
• AI agents connect through MCP using a unique card URL.
• Agents spend within defined limits.
• Users can freeze, revoke, or permanently disable cards at any time.

Key controls include:

• Spending budget
• Expiry date
• Merchant allowlist
• Per-transaction spending cap
• Delegation through sub-cards

About:

### **Velo402: The Autonomous Agent Wallet for Sui**

**Velo402** is a capability-scoped, autonomous wallet infrastructure designed to solve the biggest security bottleneck for AI agents operating on-chain: *trustless capital delegation*.

By combining Sui's object-oriented Move language with Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs) and Yield-bearing primitives, Velo402 allows humans to safely provision AI agents with spending power, strict operational boundaries, and built-in yield generation—all without ever handing over a private key.

### **The Problem**

As AI agents move from read-only chatbots to autonomous actors that execute trades, purchase data, and manage portfolios, humans face a critical security dilemma. Giving an AI agent access to a traditional wallet private key is a catastrophic security risk. However, requiring a human to manually sign every transaction an AI agent wants to make completely defeats the purpose of autonomy.

### **Our Solution**

Velo402 introduces a **Treasury & PolicyCap Architecture** built natively on Sui:

- **The Treasury:** A shared Move object that securely holds the capital. It is strictly controlled by an `OwnerCap` held safely in the human's hardware wallet.

- **The PolicyCap:** A revocable, non-transferable capability object minted by the human and given to the AI agent's throwaway keypair. The `PolicyCap` strictly enforces the agent's permissions directly at the smart contract level.

If an agent goes rogue or its key is compromised, it cannot drain the treasury. It can only execute the specific actions, within the specific budgets, approved by its `PolicyCap`. And with a single click, the human operator can trigger a Kill Switch to permanently burn the agent's capability.

### **Key Features**

- **Strict Move Execution Boundaries:** Agent permissions (such as `max_spend`, `expiration_epoch`, and `allowed_scopes`) are enforced on-chain. There are no off-chain databases to spoof.

- **Autonomous Yield Sweeping (Scallop Integration):** Idle capital sitting in the Agent's Treasury shouldn't go to waste. Velo402 automatically sweeps idle SUI into **Scallop**, ensuring the agent's funding continuously generates yield until it is needed for an operation.

- **On-Chain Agent Actions (DeepBook & L402):** Agents use their provisioned capital to autonomously interact with DeFi. Velo402 agents are equipped to execute trades on **DeepBook** (Spot, Margin, and Predict pools) and purchase premium verifiable datasets via L402 protocols.

- **Verifiable Compute (Nautilus & TEEs):** For highly sensitive operations, the `PolicyCap` can enforce `attested_compute_required`. This ensures the transaction can only execute if accompanied by a cryptographically verified AWS Nitro Enclave hash, guaranteeing the AI model's code was not tampered with.

- **Mission Control Dashboard:** A sleek, real-time command center for the human operator. Powered by Sui Server-Sent Events (SSE), the dashboard provides a live audit feed of every autonomous agent action, tracks Scallop yield, and monitors remaining agent budgets.

### **Tech Stack**

- **Smart Contracts:** Sui Move

- **DeFi Integrations:** Scallop SDK (Yield), DeepBook (Trading)

- **Verifiable Compute:** Nautilus (TEE Attestations)

- **Frontend:** Next.js 14, React, Server-Sent Events (SSE)

- **RPC Infrastructure:** Suiscan & Mysten Labs Node Architecture

Velo402 bridges the gap between Web3 and AI, proving that autonomous agents can manage capital safely, efficiently, and completely on-chain.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/ad2eff4e-943c-4be2-84e0-3d5d3ba7df3f/a35cc7a3-4059-4918-8b46-ac2a924cec7c.png

About:

**Warden — Autonomous Agent Wallet**

Letting an AI agent hold a private key is an all-or-nothing decision. If that key leaks, or the agent's logic has a bug, or it just makes a bad call under pressure, there's no ceiling on the damage it can do. Most teams either avoid autonomous trading agents entirely because of this, or they hand over full control and just hope nothing goes wrong.

Warden was built to close that gap entirely.

It's an on-chain authority framework that moves the trust boundary off the agent's code and onto the Move VM itself. Instead of giving an agent a wallet, a user deploys a WardenPolicy, a Move object that hard-codes exactly how much the agent can spend, which protocols it's allowed to interact with, and when its authority runs out. The agent doesn't get to decide its own limits. The contract does.

The agent itself runs as a standalone process that watches volatility signals from Sui's DeepBook Predict oracle. Each cycle, it pulls settled market prices, calibrates a volatility surface, and compares implied volatility against realized volatility to decide whether to sell volatility, hold, or buy a hedge. When it decides to trade, it builds one Programmable Transaction Block that does four things in sequence: checks the policy and deducts the budget, executes the DeepBook swap, consumes a receipt object, and writes a log entry. All four happen atomically, either the whole thing lands together or none of it does.

Three guarantees are enforced purely at the contract level, not trusted to the agent's behavior:

- **Budget ceiling:** the agent can never spend more than what's left in the policy, decremented on every single trade.

- **Protocol whitelist:** the agent can only call addresses that were explicitly approved when the policy was created.

- **Expiry:** the policy simply stops authorizing trades after a fixed timestamp, no owner intervention required.

What makes this enforcement airtight is something called a hot potato pattern. The receipt the policy enforcer returns has no Move abilities, meaning it can't be stored, dropped, or transferred. The only way the transaction succeeds is if that receipt gets consumed in the same transaction block, which forces the budget check, the trade, and the log write to happen together, every time, with no in-between state where a trade executed but wasn't checked.

The owner isn't locked out while any of this runs. A live dashboard shows the volatility gauge, trade count, and most recent action, refreshed every 10 seconds from chain. A full activity log shows every trade the agent has ever made, with the complete reasoning behind each one. And if anything goes wrong, a single-transaction kill switch destroys the policy object outright. Every trade attempt after that fails immediately at the Move VM level, no recovery path, no partial state left behind.

Warden is live on Sui testnet right now, running real volatility signals and submitting real on-chain trades, with the agent's authority defined entirely by what the Move VM is willing to allow.

About:

### **Vouch**

**Shareable links that let an AI agent act on your behalf - bounded by Move, revoked in one tap.**

### **The Problem:**

The agentic web has a delegation problem. AI agents that need to act on someone's behalf face a binary choice today: require a human signature for every action (no autonomy, no scale), or hold unbounded keys (no safety, no trust). Neither is acceptable.

There is no clean primitive for "this agent may do exactly this much, for exactly this long, and I can take it back instantly."

### **The Solution:
**

Vouch introduces Capability Objects, a Sui-native primitive that encodes exactly what an AI agent is allowed to do. A capability specifies:

- Asset scope

- Spending limits

- Schedule

- Allowed trading pools

- Risk rules

- Expiry conditions

All constraints are enforced on-chain by Move, not by trusting the agent.

The key architectural insight is that the agent never custodies funds. Funds remain inside a Vault object. The agent only proposes transactions, and the capability object allows execution only if every rule passes.

If the agent key is compromised, the attacker can only perform the exact actions permitted by the capability. Users can revoke permissions instantly.

### Core Security Invariants

**Every execution is enforced by Move assertions:**

- The agent cannot exceed the approved budget.

- The agent cannot trade outside approved pools.

- The agent cannot act after revocation or expiry.

- Every action is logged on-chain.

- Recipients never need a seed phrase, wallet installation, or gas.

### **Demo: Delegated DCA**

A crypto-native user wants to onboard a non-crypto recipient into SUI.

The creator writes a natural language instruction:

"$50 a week into SUI for the next two months, skip if SUI drops more than 5% in the last hour."

An LLM converts this into a structured capability configuration. The creator funds a vault and shares a link.

The recipient opens the link, signs in with Google using zkLogin, and receives a Sui address without installing a wallet or paying gas.

Before every scheduled purchase, the executor:

- Reads live Pyth oracle prices

- Reads DeepBook v3 orderbook depth

- Evaluates configured risk rules

- Executes or skips the trade

If a trade is skipped, an LLM generates a human-readable explanation that is logged on-chain.

**Example:**

"SUI dropped 6.2% in the last hour, exceeding your 5% threshold. This week's buy has been deferred."

The recipient can revoke permissions at any time. Future executions immediately fail on-chain, and remaining funds are returned atomically.

### Why This Is More Than a Cron Job

Traditional recurring purchases execute regardless of market conditions.

Vouch evaluates risk before every execution:

- Price-drop thresholds using Pyth

- Slippage estimation using DeepBook orderbook depth

When a rule is triggered:

- The trade is skipped

- The schedule advances

- Funds remain untouched

- A reason is generated and logged on-chain

This gives users visibility into why the agent paused rather than blindly executing.

### Why Sui

- Capability Objects provide first-class delegated authority.

- Atomic Programmable Transaction Blocks allow assert → withdraw → trade → log in one transaction.

- zkLogin enables Google sign-in with no wallet installation.

- Sponsored gas removes onboarding friction.

- DeepBook v3 provides on-chain orderbook data for real slippage estimation.

### Beyond DCA

The Capability Object primitive supports:

- Teen spending allowances with merchant restrictions

- Treasury sub-accounts with bounded authority

- Recurring gifts

- Agent-to-agent commerce

- Autonomous operational spending

*"The best infrastructure is invisible."*

Edge is programmable trust infrastructure for autonomous AI agents, built on Sui's native primitives — zkLogin for invisible onboarding, sponsored transactions for gasless execution, PTBs for atomic policy enforcement, and Walrus for decentralized audit storage.

The core primitive is EdgePass — a Move object encoding a complete spending policy on-chain. Budget, approved merchants, auto-approve threshold, escalation threshold, expiry. The Sui VM enforces it. A compromised agent cannot bypass it.

The TypeScript SDK ships with simulate(), budget helpers, React hooks, a full events system, and withPolicy() for wrapping any AI tool in one line. 34 passing tests. 3,500+ weekly downloads.

Live demo: an AI agent runs autonomously against a real EdgePass on Sui mainnet. Approved transactions execute on-chain. Blocked merchants are rejected in under a millisecond — never touching the chain. Escalated transactions pause for human approval. Policy encrypted with Seal. Audit trail wired into Walrus.

The agent decides. EdgePass enforces. The chain is the guarantee.

About:

### **SuiShield**

SuiShield is a policy-bound gas sponsorship agent for Sui applications. It helps dApps provide gasless onboarding while protecting the sponsor wallet from duplicate requests, excessive gas usage, unsafe retries, RPC failures, and budget exhaustion.

Before sponsoring a transaction, SuiShield checks whether the action is allowed, the user is eligible, the request is duplicated, the gas cost is within limits, the sponsor budget is sufficient, and the RPC endpoint is healthy. Based on these checks, the agent can approve, reject, pause, switch RPC endpoints, or activate Protective Mode.

Each request follows four stages: **Observe, Reason, Act, and Result**. The agent reads transaction and network conditions, applies deterministic policies, performs the appropriate action, and records the final outcome in an auditable trace.

SuiShield uses a secure two-phase architecture. In the Prepare phase, the server validates the request, checks eligibility, estimates gas, builds the transaction, and dry-runs the exact bytes. In the Execute phase, the user signs the prepared transaction, the sponsor co-signs it on the server, and both signatures are submitted to Sui. The sponsor private key never enters the browser.

The project also includes zkLogin for Google-based onboarding, a Move contract that mints one Starter Badge per wallet, duplicate protection, RPC health monitoring, failover logic, Protective Mode, transaction logs, and incident reports.

The Demo Lab demonstrates six scenarios: normal success, duplicate request, RPC failure, network instability, budget exhaustion, and Move abort. Only the normal success scenario performs a real on-chain transaction, while the failure scenarios are controlled simulations.

SuiShield does not replace the Sui network or remove gas fees. Its purpose is to protect the application layer by making sponsored transactions safer, more transparent, auditable, and easier for new users.

About:

### **MIASMA**

Memory-action quarantine for agentic Sui actions.

MIASMA protects users before an AI agent turns poisoned memory into an on-chain action.

Most systems check the transaction. MIASMA checks the memory that caused it.

Map. Verify. Block. Funds moved: 0.

### **WHAT IT IS**

MIASMA is a pre-execution security system for agentic Sui actions. It checks the memory-action path behind an AI agent’s proposed action before wallet approval, before signature, and before Sui submission.

If the path is contaminated, MIASMA suppresses the wallet request, blocks execution, and records a clean quarantine result instead of letting a dangerous transaction reach the user.

### **THE PROBLEM**

AI agents are moving from chat into execution. They can prepare payments, call tools, interact with wallets, and trigger on-chain actions.

The final transaction may look safe. The amount can look normal. The recipient can look valid. The wallet preview can look clean.

But the reason the action was created may already be poisoned.

A malicious instruction can hide upstream inside memory, policy files, tool context, previous agent state, or repeated draft behavior. By the time the wallet asks for approval, the attack may have already passed through the agent’s decision path.

Checking only the transaction is too late.

### **HOW IT WORKS**

- The agent proposes a Sui action.

- MIASMA builds a MemoryActionContext from the intent, skill, recipient, amount, memory path, and memory hash.

- The Memory-Action Map reconstructs the causal path behind the action.

- A local Rust verifier checks for hidden instruction contamination, policy drift, tool-context contamination, and suspicious draft behavior.

- If the risk score exceeds the threshold, the Skill Firewall blocks the action before wallet approval.

- MIASMA emits an Evidence Capsule and a clean quarantine record.

- The dangerous action is never signed, never submitted, and never receives a danger-action digest.

### **HOW MIASMA USES SUI**

MIASMA uses Sui as the public receipt and audit layer for blocked agentic decisions.

The dangerous action is not submitted. That is the point.

Instead, MIASMA separates the poisoned action path from the clean evidence path. The poisoned action is quarantined before wallet approval, while the clean evidence path can produce a Sui Move QuarantineReceipt with the artifact hash, proposed amount, contamination score, and `funds_moved = 0`.

This creates an auditable record of why the action was blocked without executing the dangerous transaction.

### **TECH STACK**

- Sui Move: QuarantineReceipt for blocked-decision records

- Rust verifier: local memory-action contamination scoring

- TypeScript: MemoryActionContext, scan artifacts, and app integration

- Skill Firewall: pre-wallet approval execution block

- Shadow execution: simulates the action while keeping real execution blocked

- Evidence Capsule: structured record of the quarantine decision

- Walrus / Seal paths: artifact reference and gated evidence path

- Groth16 proof path: threshold-based quarantine verification path

### **RESULT**

Projected exposure: 1,000 USDC
Memory path: vendor_policy_v3.txt -> payment_rules md-> send_usdc
Detected: hidden instruction contamination
Wallet request: suppressed
Signature: not requested
Sui submit: not submitted
Danger action digest: none
Funds moved: 0

The transaction looked valid. The memory path was poisoned.

MIASMA blocked the action before wallet approval.

### **WHY IT MATTERS**

As AI agents become capable of executing financial actions, checking only the final transaction is not enough. The attack may already be upstream.

MIASMA protects the execution boundary where agent memory becomes on-chain action.

Map. Verify. Block. Funds moved: 0.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/bbf720db-d719-4b62-aa78-dd8d45296457/6846e2c3-8c77-4f3b-a504-7faeb4c1bcc6.png

About:

**Argus — Autonomous Risk Guardian for Sui DeFi**

DeFi lending pools rarely fail because the smart contract is broken. They fail because humans are too slow. When SUI crashes 20% in a matter of minutes, nobody is watching closely enough to react in time. By the time a team notices, checks the dashboard, and decides what to do, bad debt has already built up and liquidations are already cascading through the pool. The damage is done before a single human decision gets made.

Argus was built to close that gap entirely.

It's an autonomous agent that watches Sui lending pools every 60 seconds, all day, every day. Each cycle, it pulls live SUI and BTC prices from Pyth Network, reads the current state of the pool directly from the chain, and loads its risk model from Walrus. From there it computes a single composite risk score out of 100, built from five factors: how fast prices are moving, the current volatility compared to recent history, whether SUI and BTC are dropping together (a strong signal of systemic risk rather than normal noise), how confident the price oracle itself is, and how heavily the pool is already borrowed against. If that score crosses a threshold, Argus acts immediately. No one approves the transaction. It just fires.

**The response works like a circuit breaker, with four escalating levels:**

- **YELLOW (score 60-74):** Argus tightens the loan-to-value ratio by 5%, giving the pool a bit more safety buffer before liquidations become a risk.

- **ORANGE (75-84):** A stronger response. LTV tightens by 10% and the total borrow cap shrinks to 75% of its previous size, both executed in a single atomic transaction. This usually fires when SUI and BTC are falling together, which is often the early sign of something bigger going on.

- **RED (85-94):** New borrowing is paused entirely. Existing positions stay open, but the pool stops taking on additional risk and buys itself time for the market to stabilize.

- **BLACK (95-100):** A full emergency halt. No deposits, no borrows, nothing. This is reserved for genuine black swan events, a SUI depeg, an oracle failure, a 30%+ crash happening in minutes, where the only correct move is to stop everything instantly.

Every one of these actions writes its complete reasoning on-chain, not just what happened, but exactly why. The full score breakdown, the price data, the LTV before and after, all permanently logged and verifiable by anyone.

The risk model driving all of this isn't hardcoded into the contract either. Its weights and thresholds live as a config blob on Walrus, so the DAO can make Argus more cautious or more aggressive simply by uploading a new blob. No redeployment, no new audit cycle required, Argus picks up the change within minutes.

And because no autonomous agent should hold unchecked power over real funds, a 2-of-3 guardian multisig sits above everything Argus does. Any guardian can propose an override, a second one confirms it, and the action is reversed. Argus only ever acts within the boundaries the DAO policy allows.

Argus is live on Sui testnet right now, watching real prices and firing real on-chain transactions, with no human in the loop and no delay between detecting risk and acting on it.
