- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/3ffa1f52-0786-4ef8-9a9b-e1b61361ad90/5151984f-64c9-414e-8494-14c1270aab78.png

About:

### Ballast

Verifiable, revocable authority for autonomous AI agents on Sui.

**Give your AI agent a mandate, not your keys.

**Documentation:** [https://jatinsahijwani.github.io/ballast/](https://jatinsahijwani.github.io/ballast/)

The problem

A DAO wants to let an AI agent manage part of its treasury. To do that today, you give the agent a private key, and a key can do anything. It can over-leverage a position, trade a market it was never meant to touch, get prompt-injected by an attacker, or quietly drain the account. You usually find out after the damage is done.

You would never hand a stranger your bank login and tell them to go trade for you. Yet that is what running an autonomous agent on real funds looks like right now. There is no standard, enforceable way to give an agent limited authority and take it back.

### What Ballast is

Ballast is a capability-based trust layer for autonomous agents on Sui. Instead of handing an agent your keys, you issue it a mandate: a Move object that carries hard limits the chain itself enforces. The agent acts through that object, so it cannot exceed, fake, or outlive what you granted, and you can revoke it at any time.

It is the capability-native answer to what Ethereum is now standardizing as ERC-8004, built around something only Sui offers natively: permissions as first-class objects.

### How it works

Ballast is built from four on-chain primitives.

**Identity.** A portable agent passport that carries the agent's reputation across apps.

**Capability.** The mandate itself. It holds the spend cap, the maximum leverage, the allowed markets, and an expiry. Every gated action runs through an enforce check that aborts the whole transaction if any rule would be broken, before anything settles.

**Attestation.** The agent's decision is signed inside a Trusted Execution Environment, an AWS Nitro Enclave integrated through Sui's Nautilus framework. The chain verifies that hardware attestation on-chain before the action is allowed. A forged or missing signature is rejected.

**Reputation.** Clean, attested actions accrue to the agent's identity, so a principal or another agent can judge an agent's track record before trusting it.

The reference flow is a DAO treasury delegating bounded trading authority to an agent. The treasury connects its own wallet, issues a mandate, and funds the agent inside it. The agent then trades on DeepBook on its own, but only within the mandate, with every decision attested by the enclave and verified on-chain.

### What is actually built, and live on testnet

This is not a mockup.
The full system is deployed and working on Sui testnet.

A real Move package covering identity, capability, attestation, and trading, deployed and then upgraded in place with the type origin preserved so existing objects keep working.

Real trades settled on DeepBook through the capability, with reputation updating on success.

A genuine AWS Nitro enclave that we built, measured, ran, and registered on-chain. The chain verified the AWS certificate chain, matched the enclave's PCR measurements, and bound the enclave's public key on-chain. After that, a trade signed by the enclave was verified on-chain and settled into a real DeepBook fill.

Every guardrail proven with real, committed transactions: over-spend, over-leverage, a disallowed market, an action after revocation, a prompt-injection attempt, and a forged attestation. Each one is rejected by the chain, and each has a transaction digest you can open in the explorer.

A TypeScript SDK, `@ballast/sdk`, that lets any team add these guardrails to their own agent in about ten lines. The attestation provider sits behind a clean interface, so the mock provider and the real Nautilus enclave are swappable with no API change.

A professional dapp front end built around the treasury delegation flow, where principal actions are signed by the connected wallet and the agent's signing key never reaches the browser.

Every package ID, the enclave object, the PCR values, and all transaction digests are listed in `DEPLOYMENTS.md` and in the documentation, each with an explorer link.

### Why it matters, and why Sui

Capital is already moving toward autonomous agents, and there is no safety layer underneath them. DAO treasuries, agent marketplaces, and funds running automated strategies all need a way to delegate authority without giving up control. Ballast is that layer.

It works this cleanly only on Sui. Here a permission is a first-class object that the chain enforces directly, rather than a promise bolted onto a token and checked off-chain. Paired with Nautilus for verifiable off-chain compute and DeepBook for real order-book trading, Sui has exactly the pieces this problem needs.

### The hard parts

A few things took real engineering, and they are documented in full.

Standing up a real AWS Nitro enclave, producing a reproducible enclave image, and verifying its attestation on-chain through the AWS certificate chain and PCR matching.

Matching the Rust and Move BCS byte layouts so the enclave's signed payload verifies correctly on-chain.

Diagnosing a DeepBook version-gating issue and fixing it by relinking and upgrading the package in place while preserving type identity, so live pools would accept our calls.

### Documentation

Ballast ships with complete documentation, written for a developer who wants to integrate it in a single day.

It covers:

An architecture overview, with system and module diagrams.

The capability model and the enforce logic, including the full abort-code reference.

The Nautilus TEE section, which walks through how trust is established end to end: building the enclave image, measuring PCRs, registering them on-chain, and verifying a live attestation. It includes the AWS Nitro deployment and trust-boundary diagrams.

A deployments page listing every package ID, the enclave object, the PCR values, and the transaction digests with explorer links.

A quickstart that takes you from installing the SDK to a working, guarded agent trade.

A full API reference, and a threat model that states plainly what Ballast protects against and what it does not.

Documentation: [https://jatinsahijwani.github.io/ballast/](https://jatinsahijwani.github.io/ballast/)

Tech stack

Sui, Move, DeepBook, Nautilus, AWS Nitro Enclaves, TypeScript, the Sui TypeScript SDK and dapp-kit, and Next.js.

### Roadmap

DeepBook Margin and Predict integration, an immutable action and audit log on Walrus, confidential strategies with Seal, zkLogin onboarding, agent-to-agent reputation, and a formal Sui standard proposal backed by an external security audit. The goal is for Ballast to become the open agent-safety standard that the Sui agent economy builds on.

About:

**SuiGuard — The Brain of Web3 Intents**

SuiGuard is an AI-powered intent engine that transforms plain English commands into safe, verified Sui transactions. Users simply type what they want ("Swap 10 SUI for USDC" or "Send 2 SUI to 0x...") and the system handles everything else through a 4-node agentic pipeline:

**Node 1 — Intent Parser:** Amazon Nova (AWS Bedrock) extracts structured intent from natural language using Zod-validated schemas with automatic model fallback (Lite → Micro → Pro).

**Node 2 — PTB Compiler:** Compiles the parsed intent into a native Sui Programmable Transaction Block using the official @mysten/sui TypeScript SDK — supporting transfers, swaps (Cetus AMM), balance queries, and staking.

**Node 3 — Guardian Engine:** A 5-tier pre-flight risk simulation that runs deterministic checks (Slippage, Oracle Staleness, Balance Utilization, Transaction Size, Address Validation) and generates a risk score before the user ever sees a confirmation screen.

**Node 4 — Executor:** Signs and submits the PTB to Sui testnet only after human approval via a dual-mode Guardian Review (Story Mode for beginners, Expert Mode for developers).

The entire pipeline is orchestrated by LangGraph.js with full observability via LangSmith. Every execution produces a verifiable Audit Trail timeline showing exactly what the AI did, how it reasoned, and what was submitted on-chain.

**Key Innovation:** No existing Web3 wallet combines natural language intent parsing with deep, pre-flight statistical risk modelling. SuiGuard unifies these into a single AI-orchestrated system that reasons, compiles, simulates, and explains — all before a single byte touches the blockchain.

About:

**ORCUS**

An autonomous trading agent on Sui that you can trust with execution: scoped, revocable, on-chain authority that settles on real DeepBook and never holds your keys.

**The problem**

AI agents are good at deciding and dangerous at executing. To let an agent trade for you today, you either hand it your private keys or sign a blanket token approval. There is no safe way to give an agent capped, revocable, on-chain authority. This is the approve wall. And the moment a trade reaches a public mempool, bots front-run and sandwich it. So an autonomous trading agent today is either unsafe or it gets extracted.

**What Orcus does**

You describe a strategy in plain English. It is encrypted in your browser before it ever leaves the page. You deposit into a Move vault on Sui along with the encrypted goal. An autonomous agent picks it up, decrypts it inside a Trusted Execution Environment on 0G Compute, computes live market indicators, decides whether to execute, writes a permanent audit receipt to 0G Storage, and then settles the swap on real DeepBook v3 liquidity. The agent never holds your keys and has zero discretion over the swap. The vault builds the trade itself, forces the recipient to be you, and enforces an independent price floor. You can revoke the agent at any time.

The result is an agent you can actually trust with execution. The decision logic stays sealed, the intent stays private until the trade is final, and the settlement is guaranteed correct by Sui's type system.

**How it works, step by step.**

1. The user states a goal in plain English in the web app. It is ECIES-encrypted in the browser. Only opaque bytes go on chain.

2. The user deposits SUI plus the encrypted goal into the Move vault (one active intent per user).

3. The agent detects the IntentSet event, reads the encrypted goal, and decrypts it with its key.

4. The agent builds a live market snapshot (price, moving averages, RSI, volatility) and sends it with the intent to 0G Compute for sealed inference inside an Intel TDX enclave.

5. The agent writes an audit receipt (market seen, decision, oracle floor, settlement venue) to 0G Storage and gets a merkle root.

6. The agent submits one Programmable Transaction Block that: calls release_for_swap on the vault (capability-gated, attestation-checked, refreshes the oracle, computes the slippage floor, returns the user's SUI plus a hot-potato ticket); swaps SUI to DBUSDC on real DeepBook v3; and calls settle_swap, which enforces the floor, sends DBUSDC to the user, and emits TradeExecuted carrying the receipt hash.

7. The user verifies the trade and the receipt in the proof viewer.

**WHY SUI (meaningful Sui integration, not a generic LLM wrapper)**

Sui is what makes this agent safe rather than just convenient. Four Sui primitives do real work:

1. Move capability object. The agent's authority is an AgentCap object scoped to execute-only. It cannot name a recipient, an amount, or a venue. Authority is an object the owner grants and can revoke, not a private key.

2. Programmable Transaction Block. Release, swap, and settle run atomically in a single transaction. There is no intermediate state for an attacker to exploit and nothing pending in a mempool to front-run.

3. Hot-potato ticket. release_for_swap returns a SwapTicket struct that has no abilities, so Move's type system makes it impossible to end the transaction without consuming it through settle_swap. settle_swap enforces the oracle floor and pays the user. The agent literally cannot keep the funds or skip the floor; if the fill is below the floor, the entire transaction reverts.

4. Real DeepBook v3. Settlement is a real central-limit-order-book fill (SUI to DBUSDC), not a mock and not an AMM, so the agent executes against genuine on-chain liquidity and price discovery.

On top of this, the agent's decision is attested on chain with an ed25519 signature the vault verifies, and the owner has a request_cancel escape hatch that locks the agent out after a cooldown. Scoped, revocable, audited, autonomous authority over funds, with no custody handover.

**WHAT IS LIVE (Sui testnet)**

Everything below is deployed and verifiable on Sui testnet (explorer: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet)).

Package ID (function calls): 0xd90464f6f643309be4f424338067bd847e0b53d258a5421585afc6b9d8823861

Package ID (original; event and type ids): 0x07e3af4c0e5389fe27b9fc2519cd5ccdfaae772085ce1a9e754aeb55519f9dc8

Vault object: 0x47e998d5b287f123e128f54b5b23f6f38a2bde1bf1fa8ad288a74d81b0b154f1

Oracle object: 0xc00b3ad57a1f0bf64bc4f51aae31d4a7820f3d13b956dc866c149c72d729b826

AgentCap: 0xda2724fb85c5a6ca2ab343c7bd0b058309921be6cd5fe889eaa31d15455009da

Settlement token DBUSDC (6 decimals): 0xf7152c05930480cd740d7311b5b8b45c6f488e3a53a11c3f74a6fac36a52e0d7::DBUSDC::DBUSDC

**HOW TO VERIFY / TEST**

1. Open the web app, switch the chain selector to Sui, and connect a Sui wallet (Slush or Suiet).

2. On the strategy page, type a plain-English goal, set the amount to at least 1 SUI (the DeepBook minimum order size), and submit. The intent is encrypted in the browser and deposited as opaque bytes.

3. The live agent picks it up within seconds, decides inside the TEE, writes the 0G Storage receipt, and settles the swap on DeepBook.

4. Check the dashboard for the DBUSDC settlement, and open the proof page to see the verifiable receipt (verdict, market inputs, oracle floor, sealed enclave, and the DeepBook settlement venue), with a link to the Sui vault on Suiscan.

5. Or open the example swap above directly on Suiscan and inspect the OrderFilled and TradeExecuted events.

About:

**MOBY: POLICY-GATED AUTONOMOUS AGENT WALLET ON SUI**

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY Moby is a non-custodial autonomous agent wallet built on Sui. The owner escrows SUI into a Move Policy Object that enforces a hard budget ceiling, a single DeepBook pool scope, and an expiry timestamp. An autonomous agent then executes a rule-based Reactive DCA strategy, reading the live DEEP/SUI order book each tick, scoring the spread from 0 to 100, and firing real taker swaps on DeepBook when conditions are met.

**HACKATHON TRACK ALIGNMENT**

- Primary Track: The Agentic Web

- Sub-track Target: Sub-track 2: Autonomous Agent Wallet

- Special Bounties: Special - DeepBook CLOB Integration

**ON-CHAIN SECURITY AND ENFORCEMENT**

Every swap routes through agent_swap, which acts as the single fund egress in the Move smart contract. The contract strictly enforces five immutable on-chain rules:

- Agent-only authorization

- Policy must be active

- Expiry timestamp not reached

- Correct DeepBook pool selection

- Transaction value stays under budget

If any of these rules are violated, the transaction aborts on-chain. The agent is mathematically incapable of overspending, accessing an unauthorized pool, or breaching the user's trust parameters.

**OWNER CONTROLS AND Vault MANAGEMENT**

The wallet owner retains absolute sovereignty over the escrowed funds at all times through three core primitives:

- revoke_policy: A permanent kill-switch that halts the agent in a single transaction with no cooldown.

- withdraw_unspent: Allows the owner to reclaim and withdraw escrowed SUI at any moment.

- close_policy: Drains the entire vault and revokes the agent's permissions in a single consolidated call.

**TRANSPARENT STRATEGY ARCHITECTURE**

Moby eliminates black-box uncertainty by introducing a fully transparent execution model with no LLM wrappers. Every single tick emits a plain-text decision displaying the current market spread, the calculated score, and the underlying rationale. To ensure economic safety, slippage protection dynamically derives the min_base_out value from the live best-ask immediately before each swap is fired.

**SUI TESTNET VERIFICATION AND PROOF OF EXECUTION**

The core business logic and atomic integrations have been successfully deployed and verified on Sui Testnet:

- Swap Transaction Hash: F86Hpob3s8yzRdroTKrUL6eS1WMk94AnUyF4ghP17WMv

- Atomic Event Logs: OrderFilled (DeepBook) and SpendRecorded (Moby) executed together within one single transaction block.

Media:
- None listed

About:

Giving an AI agent the ability to trade on your behalf usually means handing it a private key — and with it, unbounded control of the account. Agent Policy Builder removes that trade-off on Sui.

The tool extends OpenZeppelin's Accounts Policy Builder framework to Sui's object-centric Move model. A developer points it at a real on-chain transaction or a constructed Programmable Transaction Block (PTB). It reads the objects, type arguments, and command sequence; asks the user to confirm the exact boundaries (which pool, how much SUI, until which epoch); and generates a Move Session Capability that encodes those limits on-chain.

The agent then operates inside that capability. Any PTB it builds that strays outside the approved pool, amount, or time window aborts at the Move runtime, not in an off-chain check that can be bypassed. The AI proposes and explains; the chain enforces.

The flow is code-first and custody-preserving. The toolkit produces unsigned PTBs and runs local permit/deny dry-runs to prove a policy allows the intended trade and rejects hostile variations before anything is signed. The user signs through their own Sui wallet. Keys never touch the agent or the backend.

This proof of concept proves the full loop end to end on one high-value scenario: bounded swap delegation on Cetus. It ships as an MCP server, an agent skill, and a local CLI, with a Move Session Capability template, a permit/deny verification harness, and a worked Cetus playbook. Additional scenarios (Scallop/Navi yield claims, Sui Kiosk operations), zkLogin sign-in, and a generalized policy code generator build on this foundation in later phases.

About:

**AgentPay: a Sui-native x402 marketplace for paid API and agent calls**

**AgentPay** lets anyone turn an API, AI tool, oracle, data endpoint, or agent service into a **pay-per-call product on Suiwith Reputation based framework**

At the center of AgentPay is a marketplace where merchants can list their product endpoint, choose a price in SUI, add a settlement wallet, and let AgentPay handle the rest. The platform automatically generates product metadata, uploads it to **Walrus**, registers the product as a Sui Tool, and exposes it as an **x402-compatible paid endpoint**.

Users and agents can then browse the marketplace, inspect the payment terms, connect a wallet, and make real paid calls. When a call succeeds, AgentPay settles the payment on Sui, mints a receipt, and updates reputation based on actual paid usage.

**What AgentPay does**

- **Marketplace for paid API products**: merchants can list endpoints that users and agents pay to call.

- **x402-compatible payment flow**: unpaid requests return payment challenges, and paid requests unlock the result.

- **Sui settlement**: payments settle on-chain using Sui objects and transactions.

- **Walrus metadata**: marketplace listings automatically store public product metadata on Walrus.

- **Receipt-backed reputation**: successful paid calls create receipts and reputation signals.

- **SDKs for developers**: client, middleware, facilitator, gateway, reputation, and Sui x402 adapter packages.

- **Testnet-ready product**: hosted frontend, marketplace, console, Railway Postgres, gateway, and facilitator are wired together.

**Why it matters**

Most APIs and AI agents today are either subscription-based, centralized, or hard to trust. AgentPay makes each call **payable, verifiable, and reputation-aware**.

A merchant can list a service without building a billing system. A user can pay only when they actually use something. An agent can discover tools, pay per call, and build trust through on-chain receipts instead of vague off-chain ratings.

About:

Velfi is an intent engine for payments on Sui. You type a plain-English goal — "split $30 with alice.vel and bob.vel," "stream 50 USDC to my designer over a week," "send 20 to mum" — and Velfi compiles it into a Sui programmable transaction block, shows a human-readable preview, and runs a guardian check that surfaces risks in plain language before anything is signed. You confirm; only then does it execute.

The guardian is the core. It catches moving a large share of your balance, paying a raw address that can't be recovered, and market slippage on swaps — explained in words, not jargon — so the agent never pushes money past a wall the user understands.

Sui makes it work: multi-recipient sends, splits, streams and schedules settle atomically in one PTB with a single approval; zkLogin gives Google sign-in with no seed phrase; SuiNS `.vel` names replace 0x addresses; DeepBook powers swaps. PTBs + zkLogin are exactly what let a natural-language agent stay non-custodial and one-tap.

Built for everyday and cross-border payments in markets like Ghana and Nigeria. Runs today in assisted-signing mode; full client-side zkLogin signing is next.

About:

**HiSui is an AI-powered conversational intent engine for the Sui blockchain. It translates plain English commands into atomic, multi-protocol Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTB) with live pre-execution risk simulation and secure Web2-friendly Google zkLogin onboarding.**

The Problem We Solve

DeFi on modern blockchains is incredibly powerful but highly fragmented. To execute a simple yield-generating strategy (e.g., swapping SUI for USDC and supplying it to a lending pool), a user must navigate between separate dApps, sign multiple sequential transactions, manage slippage, and pay separate gas fees. This complexity, combined with the friction of managing seed phrases, prevents mainstream Web2 adoption.

HiSui solves this by introducing a natural language interface for Web3. Users type what they want to achieve in plain English, and HiSui compiles, simulates, and executes the entire workflow.

**Key Features**

- **Conversational AI Intent Parsing**: Powered by Google Gemini AI, HiSui parses intents such as *"Swap 0.5 SUI for USDC and deposit it in NAVI"* and maps them into structured execution steps.

- **Atomic Multi-Protocol Bundling**: Compiles all actions into a single **Programmable Transaction Block (PTB)**. Swapping on Cetus and depositing on NAVI happen in **one atomic on-chain transaction** with a single click, saving gas and time.

- **The Guardian (Live Pre-Execution Simulation)**: Before a user signs, the Guardian runs an on-chain dry-run. It verifies execution success, fetches live prices from the **Pyth Network** oracle to check trade fairness, calculates execution rates, and highlights safety checkmarks.

- **Secure Web2-friendly Google zkLogin**: Users log in instantly with their Google account. Ephemeral key management, 16-byte salt caching, and ZK proving are handled entirely in the background, removing the need for seed phrases.

**How It Works**

1. **Authentication**: The user logs in via Google OAuth. HiSui handles the zkLogin cryptographic loop (generating the ephemeral keypair, caching the 16-byte user salt, and requesting zero-knowledge proofs from the Mysten Labs/Shinami provers).

2. **Intent Input**: The user types a goal in the floating input box.

3. **Parsing & Compilation**: An Express security proxy forwards the prompt to Google Gemini AI, returning a structured JSON action plan. The frontend compiler converts this plan into a single Sui transaction block, dynamically splitting and preparing coins.

4. **Simulation & Check**: The compiled transaction is simulated on-chain. The Guardian service compares the simulated trade outputs against Pyth Oracle prices.

5. **Execution**: The user clicks "Approve & Sign". The transaction is signed using the zkLogin keypair and broadcast to the SUI network, returning a single transaction digest.

Integrated Protocols & Technologies

- **Cetus Protocol**: Deep-liquidity swap routing.

- **NAVI Protocol**: Liquidity lending deposits.

- **Pyth Network**: SUI/USD real-time price feeds.

- **Shinami / Mysten Labs**: Zero-knowledge proving service.

- **Google Gemini API**: Natural language intent parsing.

- **Sui zkLogin**: Web2-native cryptographic keys.

- **Core Stack**: React (Vite), TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Node.js (Express proxy).

About:

Omen is the missing accountability layer for Sui's agentic economy.

Every AI agent operating on Sui today acts without a verified human

behind it. When an agent causes damage, there's no on-chain way to

trace responsibility, apply consequences, or prevent it happening

again.

Omen fixes this with three primitives: a soulbound OmenBadge

that gives human founders a permanent, unforgeable identity; an

AgentBadge that cryptographically tethers every AI agent to its

verified founder; and an automatic slash mechanism that cascades

consequence from agent to human the moment misbehavior is detected no committee vote, no appeal, no escape.

This isn't simulated. Two OmenBadges and three AgentBadges are live

on Sui testnet right now, including one that was deliberately slashed

to prove the kill switch actually fires on-chain verifiable transaction

included.

Agent logic is stored on Walrus and referenced directly in

each badge.

The entire frontend reads live from the Sui RPC with zero

backend dependency paste any address and get a real verdict in under

two seconds.

Built solo, self-taught, in two weeks, by a student building from a

campus in Nigeria, first Sui hackathon, first Move contracts ever

written.

Live demo, full on-chain proof, and source code: [[https://github.com/omenprotocol/omen-landing-page](https://github.com/omenprotocol/omen-landing-page/tree/main)]

- **Checkpoint-Level Scanning**: Continuously monitors transaction blocks block-by-block, parsing state transitions and balances in sync with network checkpoints to secure low latency.

- **Package Upgrade Safeguards**: Instantly alerts protocol teams when package upgrade capabilities are utilized by non-whitelisted addresses.

- **AI-Powered Telemetry Rules**: Automatically analyzes Sui Move smart contracts using LLMs to generate customized security rules and parameters.

- **Incident Response Timeline**: Aggregates telemetry signals and alerts into categorized incidents to eliminate notification fatigue.

- **Nordic Minimalist Dashboard**: A premium, clean dashboard console displaying live mainnet operational health and threat vectors.

Media:
- None listed

About:

**Histograph is a Web3 intelligence platform that explains the story behind blockchain activity.**

Blockchains show the action: a wallet moved funds, swapped tokens, upgraded a package, or voted on a proposal. Histograph connects that action with related signals from X, GitHub, governance activity, audit reports, and other sources, then turns everything into a structured intelligence record called a **Capsule**.

A Capsule explains what happened, which actors are involved, what evidence supports the event, how confident the system is, and why the activity may matter.

Histograph uses multiple models:

- **Pattern model:** detects suspicious behavior sequences, such as rug pulls, insider-style trading, coordinated pump campaigns, smart-money exits, wash trading, and exploit patterns.

- **Security model:** reads commits, audit text, and security-related reports to identify security-relevant activity.

- **Sentiment model:** reads market and social text to classify whether posts are bullish, bearish, or neutral.

- **Entity system:** connects wallets, handles, and identities using proof-backed links instead of guessing.

- **Context model:** turns the structured Capsule data into plain-English summaries.

The pattern model was trained and tested with curated Web3 cases, including:

- Sui exploit and incident cases, such as Cetus and Scallop-style exploits

- Rug-pull sequence examples

- Coordinated pump campaign examples

- Smart-money exit examples

- Wash-trading cluster examples

- Clean negative cases, like normal liquidity migrations and audit-only events, so the system does not flag everything as suspicious

Histograph also preserves evidence and model artifacts with Walrus-backed storage. It hashes and stores evidence bundles so Capsules can be reviewed later with their supporting sources intact.

Histograph helps agents, trading systems, compliance teams, and protocol operators understand not just what happened on-chain, but the context around it.

Trepa is an AI-powered financial assistant built on Sui that lets users interact with DeFi using plain English instead of complex blockchain tools. A user can simply say, “Invest 100 USDC conservatively,” and Trepa automatically creates the right on-chain transaction, checks for risks like high slippage or low liquidity, explains those risks in simple terms, and asks for approval before executing.

What makes Trepa unique is that it doesn't just execute transactions it builds and manages its own treasury. Revenue generated through the platform is reinvested to earn yield, and that yield funds future agent operations. This means Trepa can sustainably operate over time while keeping user funds protected and every action verifiable on-chain.

About:

**Aether is the machine execution layer for Physical AI.
**

Built on Sui, x402, MQTT, and Walrus, **Aether **lets AI agents discover real hardware capabilities, pay or authorize actions, dispatch commands to ESP32, Jetson, and robotic endpoints, and receive verifiable execution proofs.

Instead of treating hardware as isolated devices with fragmented APIs, Aether turns machine actions into **programmable, accountable, machine-to-machine transactions** — creating the foundation for agent-controlled robotics, IoT, industrial automation, and Physical AI networks.

About:

**Mika - Attention Layer for AI Agents on Sui**

**AI agents earn USDC while thinking. Advertisers get targeted attention on-chain.**

Mika is a decentralized ads layers for ai agents where advertisers bid for placement inside AI coding tools, and developers/agents earn passively from their attention.

**How It Works**

- Advertisers create campaigns on Sui, upload creatives to Walrus, and bid in the on-chain auction.

- Earners install the extension - it displays the winning ad during Claude Code, Cursor, or Copilot thinking/loading states.

- After 15s view or click, the agent signs the even and sends it to the server.

- Server batches and settles via Sui PTB every 15s (50% to earner, 50% treasury). Claim USDC anytime from escrow.

**Key Highlights**

- zkLogin + zero gas for earners

- Walrus for decentralized creatives

- Full on-chain transparency & auditability

- Agents earn → can pay for their own compute

About:

OpsFlow — the first AI agent with self-enforcing safety, built for Sui Overflow 2026 (Agentic Web track).

OpsFlow is a policy-bound AI operations agent: natural-language procurement and payout requests become stateful Sui workflows with deterministic policy checks, human approvals, autonomous execution under bounded onchain authority, and a verifiable audit trail.

The core idea: the LLM only proposes. Every state transition, every policy check, every dollar moved goes through deterministic code mirrored by Move asserts onchain — and the agent's own authority (AgentCap) is a revocable Sui object, not a config flag.

What this demo shows:

- Natural-language intake with a live, step-by-step agent reasoning trace

- Self-approval blocked by onchain role checks (Alice can't approve her own request)

- Autonomous execution under a bounded, revocable AgentCap

- Graduated vendor trust — a first-time payee carries full risk weight; that risk decays as the agent builds an executed track record, disappearing after three clean payments

- Threshold-structuring detection — splitting payments to dodge approval thresholds gets caught and escalated

- A timed veto window on high-risk payments — even after two humans approve, funds wait for a challenge window before moving

- Circuit-breaker self-suspension — three incidents in ten minutes and the agent revokes its own onchain authority and waits for a human, no one has to pull the plug

- An independent auditor agent (maker-checker) that has to agree with every decision before a human is even asked

- A verifiable audit trail — every execution receipt anchors a hash of the parsed intent, fired rules, plan, and approvals

Live demo: [https://opsflow-hso3.onrender.com](https://opsflow-hso3.onrender.com) Repo: [https://github.com/aleksarriola-max/opsflow](https://github.com/aleksarriola-max/opsflow) Sui testnet package: 0xe79f4b0499f640503a3842897667d4dd4900643397f5065daa09ad51bd9d8b2e Explorer: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xe79f4b0499f640503a3842897667d4dd4900643397f5065daa09ad51bd9d8b2e](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xe79f4b0499f640503a3842897667d4dd4900643397f5065daa09ad51bd9d8b2e)

Timestamps: 0:00 The problem and the golden rule: the LLM proposes, deterministic code decides 0:18 Natural-language intake and the live agent reasoning stream 0:38 Approval and onchain execution 0:55 Vendor trust that learns 1:15 Threshold-structuring detection 1:30 Veto window on high-risk payments 1:48 The circuit breaker: agent self-suspension 2:17 Close

Built with: Sui Move (4 modules — Org/roles, PolicySet/BudgetBucket, WorkflowRequest state machine, AgentCap), Node/TypeScript backend (deterministic policy engine, anomaly detection, auditor pass, NL policy compiler with backtest), React frontend. zkLogin walletless approvals and Coin<T>-generic USDC payouts are both shipped, not roadmap.

About:

### ***Pay Per Card***

**PayPer Card turns Sui USDC into controlled virtual spending power for AI agents.**

An agent requests a merchant and spending limit. PayPer Card responds with an HTTP 402 payment requirement, the agent signs a Sui USDC transaction, and the server verifies and settles it on-chain. Once confirmed, Lithic issues a merchant-locked virtual card with the approved limit.

This bridges crypto-native agents with businesses that still accept only traditional card payments, without exposing a permanent card or allowing unrestricted spending.

### How It Works

- An agent requests a card for a specific merchant and amount.

- The API returns an x402 Sui USDC payment challenge.

- The agent signs the transaction using its Sui wallet.

- PayPer Card self-facilitates verification and settlement through Sui RPC.

- Lithic provisions a merchant-locked, single-transaction virtual Visa card.

- The API returns the card and on-chain settlement receipt.

### Use Cases

- Autonomous cloud infrastructure payments

- Agent-managed SaaS subscriptions

- Travel and procurement agents

- Controlled API-to-card payments

- Human-approved purchasing workflows

- Spending-limited wallets for autonomous systems

### Technical Stack

- Sui Testnet and native USDC

- x402 v2 payment flow

- Self-hosted Sui facilitator with transaction dry-run validation

- Next.js App Router APIs and SSR

- Lithic sandbox card issuance

- Vercel-ready deployment

- CLI and visual agent demos

### Milestones Completed

- End-to-end Sui USDC settlement

- Automatic HTTP 402 challenge, signing and retry

- Self-facilitation without relying on an unavailable public Sui facilitator

- Merchant-locked virtual card provisioning

- On-chain transaction receipts

- Responsive web demo and CLI buyer

- Production build and Vercel configuration

**PayPer Card gives agents access to the existing card economy while keeping every purchase bounded, auditable and settled on Sui.**

About:

Most “AI wallets” are chat windows glued to an RPC. You still paste addresses, still pray the model didn’t hallucinate the amount, and keys either live in a hosted dashboard or nowhere useful at all.

**Tychi is a self-custody agentic wallet for Sui mainnet** — built for the moment agents actually hold money and need guardrails, not demos.

You talk in plain language. Tychi’s brain turns intent into structured actions. **Policy runs before anything signs.** When real money moves, you confirm. **Signing stays on your machine** — mnemonics and private keys never touch the LLM or the hosted brain.

**Two ways to use it today:**

**1. REPL (**`@tychilabs/tyi-sui`**)** — the operator console. Run it locally, onboard once, manage portfolio and sends from a terminal you control. Create/import wallets, switch accounts, set spending caps and blocked addresses, review history. This is the custody layer — the part we don’t open-source, because it’s the product.

**2. MCP (**`@tychilabs/tyi-sui-mcp`**)** — the agent bridge. Nine stdio tools for Cursor, Claude Desktop, OpenClaw, or any MCP host. Agents route intent, check readiness, onboard wallets, then drive `tyi_chat` for portfolio oversight and policy-governed SUI/USDC transfers. Hosts orchestrate; they never receive your keys.

**What’s live in beta (mainnet):**

- Portfolio view across **SUI. USDC and other coins** on your active wallet

- Sends with **estimate → confirm → execute** (no silent broadcasts)

- **Policy kernel** — per-tx/session caps, blocked recipients, custom rules

- **Multi-wallet** keystore (BIP39 or Sui private key import)

- Transaction history you can query back through the agent

**What we’re building next on Sui:** on-chain Move spending caps, owner revoke, DeepBook under the same confirm-and-sign loop, Walrus + Seal for encrypted agent memory.

Tychi isn’t a hackathon UI. It’s wallet infrastructure for the agentic web — autonomy where policy allows, confirmation where risk demands it, custody always local.

`1.0.0-beta.1` · Apache-2.0 (MCP) · npm install, no custody surrender.

About:

Aegis is an AI-native intent engine that turns plain-English DeFi goals into guarded, executable transactions on Sui.

Users describe an intent such as “Swap 1 SUI to USDC with maximum 1% slippage.” OpenAI gpt-4o-mini converts it into a strictly validated SUI/DBUSDC intent. Aegis then reads live DeepBook market data and deterministically evaluates four risk classes: price impact, liquidity depth, market spread, and data freshness.

Before signing, users receive a human-readable Programmable Transaction Block preview containing the policy, pool, amounts, minimum output, DEEP fee budget, risk verdict, and exact Move call sequence. The transaction is dry-run against Sui and requires explicit user confirmation. Warning-level transactions require an additional risk acknowledgement, while blocked, fallback, stale, and stress-mode plans cannot execute.

Sui is Aegis’s security layer, not merely its payment rail. A user-owned GuardianPolicy Move object enforces spending ceilings, maximum slippage, allowed DeepBook pool, ownership, expiration, and persistent revocation. The final wallet-signed PTB atomically performs three operations:

1. Assert the GuardianPolicy

2. Execute a real DeepBook swap

3. Mint an on-chain IntentReceipt and emit an IntentExecuted event

If any operation fails, the entire transaction reverts.

Aegis is live on Sui testnet with an Explorer-verifiable package, real DeepBook execution, active and revoked policy proofs, owned receipt objects, transaction history, and analytics.

About:

**Talos: autonomous yield management, proved on chain.**

Stablecoin lending rates on Sui move every block. Most people park their funds in one protocol and leave yield on the table, because nobody can watch a dozen dashboards twenty-four hours a day. The obvious fix is to let an AI agent manage it for you, but handing an autonomous agent real money is terrifying. What stops it from draining the wallet, chasing a bad venue, or acting on a hallucinated decision?

Talos answers that with two autonomous agents and an on-chain guardrail the agent physically cannot break.

Icarus, the executor, reads live USDC lending rates across Scallop, Navi, and Kai every thirty seconds, picks the best venue with an LLM (gpt-oss-120b), and rebalances real USDC on Sui mainnet. It can only ever spend through an on-chain Move policy: a shared object that enforces a budget ceiling, a per-transaction cap, a protocol allowlist, and an expiry. That check runs inside the same Programmable Transaction Block as the trade, so if any bound is exceeded the entire rebalance reverts atomically. The human owner holds a capability object (OwnerCap) and can revoke, top up, or extend the policy at any time, while the agent holds nothing that can override it.

Daedalus, the critic, independently re-judges every move Icarus makes, scores it from 0 to 100 with a reason, and writes that verdict to an on-chain reputation ledger signed by its own separate key. No agent trusts another. The chain is the referee, and anyone can verify it on Suiscan.

Every decision, including the agent's reasoning, is stored on Walrus as content-addressed, public memory. Talos runs continuously on its own infrastructure and survives reboots, and every action it takes links to its Suiscan transaction and its Walrus record.

This is not a backtest or a mockup. It has been running unattended on Sui mainnet for days, with thousands of decision cycles and hundreds of policy-gated rebalances, every one inside its policy bounds, and every rebalance independently graded on-chain by a second agent. We are not here to show returns. We are here to show that autonomous financial agents can be made safe and verifiable, using Sui's object model, capabilities, and atomic transactions in a way that would be clumsy or impossible elsewhere.

**Built on Sui:** custom Move contracts (agent policy and reputation), shared objects, the object-capability pattern, atomic Programmable Transaction Blocks, on-chain events, and Walrus for verifiable agent memory.

About:

FRX Labs is building the security infrastructure layer for autonomous AI agents on Sui.

As AI agents become capable of managing assets, executing strategies, and interacting with DeFi protocols, a critical challenge emerges: how can agents operate autonomously without requiring constant human approval while preventing unsafe or malicious actions?

FRX Labs introduces **FRX Shield**, an AI-powered security layer that enables safe autonomous execution through intelligent risk analysis, programmable policies, and on-chain enforcement.

FRX Shield acts as a trust layer between AI agents and blockchain execution. Before an agent performs an action, FRX Shield evaluates the request, verifies permissions, analyzes risks, and determines whether execution should proceed.

The system enables:

- **Autonomous Risk Guardian:** AI-powered monitoring of DeFi environments that detects abnormal market conditions, evaluates risk scores, and can trigger protocol protection actions through Sui Move policy objects.

- **Intent Guardian:** Converts user goals into executable Sui transaction flows while analyzing risks such as slippage, liquidity issues, and execution risks before user confirmation.

- **Agent Security Infrastructure:** Provides developers with APIs to integrate FRX Shield into any autonomous agent, enabling controlled execution without giving agents unrestricted wallet access.

FRX Wallet is the reference application built on top of FRX Shield. It demonstrates how an autonomous AI trading agent can operate on Sui by allowing users to allocate funds, define strategies, and execute trades through DeepBook while FRX Shield enforces security policies, budget limits, and risk controls.

FRX Labs leverages Sui-native primitives including **Move Objects** for programmable security policies and agent identities, **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs)** for secure autonomous workflows, and **DeepBook** for decentralized agent execution.

FRX Labs is creating the missing security layer required for the autonomous web — enabling AI agents to become powerful on-chain actors without sacrificing user control, transparency, or safety.

- Created: 2026-06-20T15:44:18.244Z
- Updated: 2026-06-20T15:44:18.244Z
- Like count: 0
- Logo: https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/projects/757d3859-ada7-4354-95f6-e55d7be9ef0b/41035e0f-f69b-4e26-a17b-d6b3f062fef8.png

Links:
- github: https://github.com/Abdol164/Qubit/
- website: https://qubit-rho.vercel.app/
- youtube: https://www.loom.com/share/7316b4effcaa4023add366386890128f

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/757d3859-ada7-4354-95f6-e55d7be9ef0b/d53a5802-cdc6-4eed-a112-42dcd925bd50.png

About:

Classical public-key cryptography  the kind securing nearly every blockchain key and signature today  is provably breakable by a sufficiently powerful quantum computer. That computer doesn't need to exist yet to be a threat: adversaries can record encrypted traffic now and decrypt it later, a strategy known as "harvest now, decrypt later." This risk compounds for autonomous agents, which will transact and coordinate on-chain continuously, at a scale and autonomy no human wallet-holder ever did, with no one watching in real time to catch a compromise.

Qubit deploys the first post-quantum key registry on Sui  any agent can build on top of it. Using ML-KEM-768, NIST's standardized post-quantum key-encapsulation mechanism, paired with AES-256-GCM for the message payload, agents publish quantum-resistant public keys to a shared Move registry object on testnet. Any other agent can look up a counterpart's key the same way it would resolve a name, establish a shared secret, and communicate securely  without re-solving the post-quantum key-exchange problem themselves.

The submission includes a working end-to-end demo: two fully autonomous agents, each its own independent process with its own Sui identity no human login, no centralized auth. Agent A registers its key on-chain, looks up Agent B's key live from the registry, encrypts an instruction, and sends it over a real network relay. Agent B receives it, decrypts it, and autonomously executes a transaction on Sui testnet  a real, verifiable transaction triggered by an instruction it only ever saw in encrypted form.

Sui's object model makes the registry a shared, composable resource any contract or agent can read and extend  turning post-quantum key discovery into a primitive, not a one-off integration every builder has to solve. As autonomous agents become first-class participants in the Agentic Web, Qubit is the trust layer underneath them.

About:

**iStudy AI** is an AI-powered A-Level exam preparation platform that delivers examiner-level feedback on student answers.

**Core features:**

- **Smart Evaluation** — paste any exam question and answer to receive detailed scoring across Knowledge, Application, Analysis, and Evaluation (KASE framework), with annotated feedback and improvement suggestions

- **Study Modes** — three adaptive learning modes: Socratic (guided questioning), Base Concepts (first-principles breakdown), and Exam Drill (mark scheme framework)

- **Analytics Dashboard** — tracks performance trends across sessions with radar charts, learner profiling, and AI-generated study method recommendations

- **OCR Upload** — extract text from handwritten or printed answer sheets via Gemini Vision

- **Decentralized History** — evaluation records stored permanently on Walrus Protocol (Sui testnet), ensuring tamper-proof learning history without centralized databases

A CrewAI agent crew analyzes live market data (BTC/SUI prices, on-chain/Dune metrics, news) and produces a confidence-scored verdict (BUY/SELL/HOLD); if approved and confidence clears a threshold, the backend autonomously signs a Sui Move transaction (via a GuardianCap) to adjust loan-to-value ratio and borrow caps, or pause the market if risk spikes. A Next.js dashboard visualizes the live risk score, prices, and on-chain action log, and lets a human/DAO holding a separate DaoCap override the market at any time — signed by their own connected wallet — to instantly reset parameters and lift any pause, acting as a manual circuit breaker on top of the autonomous agent.

About:

### SuiSage: The Guardrail Layer for AI-Driven DeFi on Sui

AI agents are starting to control real money on-chain, and almost nothing stops a careless one from torching a wallet by buying a rug, filling on a stale oracle price, or dumping into a pool too thin to absorb the trade. SuiSage is the layer that stands between that trade and your funds.

### How It Works

You tell it what you want in plain English — like *"swap 200 USDC for SUI."* SuiSage compiles it into a transaction, then runs four live risk checks against a risk policy that lives on-chain, not in our code:

- **Slippage**

- **Oracle staleness**

- **Pool concentration**

- **Token risk**

It shows you a plain-English receipt with every danger flagged, and it won't let you sign until you've seen them.

When a trade is genuinely dangerous, SuiSage doesn't just warn you ,it blocks it in Move, at the Sui VM level, where no agent and no buggy code can slip past. Even a refusal is attested on-chain, in the same transaction as the assessment.

### Agent Guardrails

When an agent runs on its own, its spending ceiling is enforced by a Move-level **BudgetCap**. Even a buggy or compromised agent physically cannot spend past what you allowed, and you can revoke its authority on-chain in a single transaction.

Every assessment , including every trade it turns down , is recorded on-chain in the same transaction as the swap, so the reasoning and the action share one digest. Nothing off-chain to forge.

### Memory & Infrastructure

All of the agent's memory lives on **Walrus**, encrypted with **Seal**, indexed on-chain (each blob is referenced from an on-chain `ActivityLog` entry), and owned by the user. Switch the AI from Claude to GPT-4 to Llama mid-session, and your full history travels with you ,because it lives on Walrus, not inside any one vendor.

The app itself is served straight from Walrus under the SuiNS name `suisage.sui`. No backend, no database, no key we hold on your behalf.

***The guardian was never meant to be a warning banner bolted onto a chatbot. In SuiSage, the guardian is the product.**

### Roadmap

The demo already proves the four primitives everything else builds on: atomic on-chain risk attestation, governed on-chain thresholds, Move-walled agent budgets, and vendor-neutral Walrus memory. None of them are single-use.

### Near Term

The same Guardian flow extends to stake/unstake, LP add+remove, and lending, each action just tunes a different corner of the check library ,plus scheduled autopilot (DCA, conditional swaps), still budget-walled in Move.

### Mid Term

Adapters for the major Sui protocols (Aftermath, Turbos, Bluefin, NAVI) under one Guardian, and a **GuardianCap** reputation primitive , where every assessment a wallet survives compounds an on-chain score other dapps can read.

### Endgame

Ship `<GuardianGate intent={...} />`, a React component any Sui dapp drops into its own swap flow to inherit the full assessment and attestation pipeline. Protocols carrying **GuardianReports** on every swap become cheaper to underwrite, so the attestation itself becomes the insurance.

### Business Model

- **Free for users**

- **B2B SaaS** for dapps embedding the GuardianGate SDK

- **Basis points on agent-executed volume** (transparent, on-chain)

- **Revenue share** with strategy authors in an agent-template marketplace

The pitch isn't a someday-vision. Each phase is the obvious extension of a primitive you can verify on-chain today.

🌐 **Live at  **[*suisage.wal.app*](http://suisage.wal.app)   everything you see is real and verifiable on-chain.

About:

Sentinel Protocol — a programmable policy wallet for autonomous AI agents on Sui. Give an AI agent a budget, a scope, and an expiry — enforced on-chain by Move, not by the app.

Sentinel lets an owner mint an on-chain AgentPolicy that delegates BOUNDED trading authority to an AI agent — a budget cap, allowed DeepBook pools, an expiry, and instant revocation. The agent places real DeepBook orders whose limits are enforced on-chain by Move. The model only proposes; the chain is the source of truth.

THE PROBLEM

AI agents hit an approval wall: every action needs a human signature, so real-time

automation is impossible. The naive fix — handing an agent a hot wallet — is the

dangerous one. A leaked key, a buggy prompt, or a runaway loop can drain everything.

WHAT WE BUILT

Sentinel Protocol is a programmable policy wallet for autonomous AI agents on Sui.

An owner mints an on-chain `AgentPolicy` object that delegates *bounded* trading

authority to an agent:

max 500 USDC · DeepBook pools only · expires 24h · revocable anytime

The agent then executes REAL DeepBook orders whose limits are enforced ON-CHAIN by

Move — not by the backend. Every action is written to an on-chain activity log, and

the owner can pause or revoke in a single signed transaction. After revocation, the

agent's very next order is rejected by the chain itself.

WHY SUI (not a generic LLM wrapper)

Our guiding principle is deterministic execution over LLM-driven transaction

construction — the model only proposes; the chain is the source of truth.

• Move objects as policy — budget is consumed by `validate_and_consume`

(package-private, only the adapter can call it). A leaked agent key still can't

exceed the cap, trade a disallowed pool, or act after expiry.

• PTBs — validate budget, place the real order, record reservation, emit event,

all in ONE Programmable Transaction Block. No partial states.

• DeepBook — orders are real on-chain calls, never simulated. Pools load from the

official registry; no hard-coded ids.

• zkLogin — owners onboard (mint / fund / pause / revoke) with no browser-extension wallet.

• On-chain events — the dashboard renders only what the chain emitted.

WHAT THE CHAIN ENFORCES (holds even if the backend is bypassed or the agent key leaks)

Budget ceiling · Per-order cap · Pool allowlist · Expiry · Pause/Revoke blocks the

next order · Adapter-only spend.

PROVEN ON TESTNET

An unattended 5-slice TWAP run walked a policy's budget down on-chain — risk climbing

LOW → MEDIUM → HIGH as the mandate was consumed — then the owner revoked and the next

order was BLOCKED (POLICY_NOT_ACTIVE). Every digest is on Sui Explorer.

Package (testnet): 0x88bcaa7dc74c1c0dbec2429a1a96e088cec566f6eb4dc8cfb7f027fe480ca035

BUILT FOR

Sui Overflow 2026 — Agentic Web, Sub-Track 2: Autonomous Agent Wallet.

Move 2024.beta · Fastify 5 + Prisma 6 · @mysten/sui + @mysten/deepbook-v3 · Next.js 16 + React 19 · zkLogin · DeepBook · Sui testnet

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7a489b3a-ef14-4d0d-8416-469b764df7c8/9e709f84-f4ac-426a-a3df-e96524b8d1b8.png

About:

**TL;DR** — An autonomous AI agent that **forecasts the short-term price *distribution*** and shapes Cetus DLMM liquidity around it (swap fees + buy-low/sell-high spread + lending on idle capital), running entirely **inside a Move permission boundary that lets it manage the position but never withdraw a coin**. It discovers its own work from on-chain delegation events — no server, no API key, the chain is the control plane. Submitted as an **open-source template** (framework + ML pipeline, not trained weights): fork it, train your own model, own your own risk.

LP Agent plugs into LeafSheep's `PositionManager` delegation slot. We submit the framework, the permission model, and the forecasting pipeline — no central service, no weights to trust. Thesis essay: *"Market Making Is a Forecasting Problem: The Design of an Open-Source LP Agent for Sui."*

- **Repo:** [github.com/RandyPen/lp-agent](https://github.com/RandyPen/lp-agent)

- **Protocol:** [LeafSheep / CDPM](https://app.leafsheep.xyz) on Sui mainnet · **License:** Apache-2.0

**Why this belongs in The Agentic Web**

- **It discovers its own work from the chain.** Subscribes to `AgentAdded` events; auto-enrolls any `PositionManager` delegated to its address; `AgentRemoved` / `PositionManagerClosed` drops it. No registration, no API key — the chain is the control plane.

- **It acts through atomic transactions.** Every decision is one Programmable Transaction Block (collect → remove → transfer → redeem → add → supply). Rebalance + lending land together or not at all.

- **Permissions enforced by a contract, not by promises.** A compromised agent key still cannot move a single coin out (see *Custody safety*).

- **It's an open agent market.** The contract enforces *what* an agent may do, not *who* it is. Any address can be authorized and compete on live performance.

**The thesis: LP is a forecasting problem**

A DLMM position is a histogram of capital across discrete price bins. The only question: *which bins will price visit in the next 30 minutes?* Most auto-rebalancers chase price *after* it moves; we forecast a **distribution, not a point** — q10 / q50 / q90 at a 30-minute horizon give center, width, and crossing probabilities, and bin weights ladder *ahead* of the flow. Load-bearing belief: for an LP, **σ matters more than μ, and σ is far more predictable** (a conservative width-only mode exists from day one).

**Architecture *(see the architecture diagram in the images below)***

The chain is the control plane: delegate a `PositionManager` on-chain, the agent auto-discovers it, and every decision leaves as **one atomic PTB** that can manage the position but never withdraw from it. The ML sidecar is optional — reachable only through the `PredictionProvider` seam, with a rule-based forecast as the always-on Tier 0 floor.

**What it does**

- **Manages custodied Position Managers.** Hard-bounded permissions — **CAN:** add/remove liquidity, collect fees, transfer fees → balance, supply/redeem to Scallop + Kai lending. **CANNOT (owner-only, on-chain):** withdraw funds, close positions, authorize/revoke agents, modify config. *(See the custody-boundary image below.)*

- **Strategies + states.** Four strategies — `singleBin`, `multiBinSpot`, `emaTrend`, `mlAgent`; three-state machine `NORMAL` / `TREND` / `EXTREME` with continuous width/bias knobs (~10 DOF). One atomic PTB per rebalance.

- **ML forecasting (pipeline in-tree, alpha yours).** Python LightGBM quantile sidecar (uv, FastAPI) → q10/q50/q90 + volatility via one `PredictionProvider`; train and serve share the same feature module. No silent degradation — timeout / drift (PSI) / stale inputs fall back to Tier 0 with a logged reason. Ships the pipeline + reproducibility, **not trained weights**.

- **Idle assets earn.** Capital far from the active price routes to **Scallop or Kai** via an APY-aware router (dust floors, per-protocol caps) instead of sitting idle.

- **Monetization (optional, off by default).** Treasury layer: per-user deposit address + off-chain credit ledger, per-action billing with auto-refund on failed PTBs, gasless stablecoin deposits.

**Custody safety — the boundary is in the contract**

Risk controls ship *before* the model; circuit breakers and audit logs *are* the product, the model is swappable alpha.

- **L1 (soft):** anomaly signals raise the lending ratio and narrow range.

- **L2 (hard):** flash crash / TVL collapse / de-anchoring / crossing-prob spike → `EXTREME`, 100% to lending.

- **L3 (emergency stop):** suspected key compromise → halt on-chain ops, monitoring only.

- **Shadow mode:** full decision path, log-only, no PTBs submitted.

- **Three identity guards** abort startup if any disagrees; agent and treasury are independent roles.

Scope: the contract guarantees **custody, not performance** — a bad strategy can still lose to IL or slippage, so deployers cap initial live capital hard.

**Validation — falsifiable gates, not backtest returns**

- **Forecast quality:** pinball loss beats the baseline with significance; the 80% interval covers 76–84% of outcomes; else the width-only path.

- **Unit economics:** every state's expected daily cost clears its fee-income budget (from real on-chain swaps, not CEX volume).

- **Shadow mode:** two weeks log-only, then real funds at a tiny cap.

PnL is an observation metric for the next iteration — never the launch gate.

**Status (honest)**

**An open-source template, not a running application** — no hosted deployment, no managed service, no live fund behind this submission.

- **Built + tested:** full framework + ML pipeline have landed — **572 Bun tests + ~100 pytest tests, typecheck clean**.

- **Not yet claimed:** not validated on a live mainnet PM; **no trained model weights ship**.

- **What a deployer owns:** their own model (or rule-only mode), fee design, capital limits, agreements, monitoring.

**Try it / verify the claims (no keys, no funds needed)**

- `bun test` (572 pass) and `cd ml && uv run pytest` (~100 pass).

- `ML_SHADOW_MODE=true bun start` runs the full decision path and logs what `mlAgent` *would* submit, **without sending a single PTB** (decisions land in `shadow_decisions`).

- Read `src/prediction/provider.ts`, `src/risk/circuits.ts`, and the CDPM contract (`0x573584cc…`).

**Tech stack & extension seams**

Bun + TypeScript + SQLite (agent); uv + Python, LightGBM + FastAPI (ML). ~16 kLOC, Apache-2.0, no Rust. Five extension seams: new strategy (`Strategy.plan()`), pool profile (`PoolProfile`), lending protocol (`LendingProtocol`), lendable coin (`LENDING_OPPORTUNITIES`), prediction model (`PredictionProvider`). A sixth — `MacroSignal` — is reserved in the design.

**Ecosystem fit**

- **Cetus DLMM** — the bin-based liquidity protocol this agent makes markets on.

- **LeafSheep / CDPM** — the `PositionManager` delegation contract (`0x573584cc4698e82fd85f2b54e64ad4cd901c42b768f7628ec167bf2d24aa2aa7`) that supplies the custody boundary.

- **Scallop + Kai** — lending yield for idle capital.

About:

**Lyra — autonomous finance on Sui, bounded by code.**

Lyra is an AI agent that performs real on-chain actions on Sui — where deterministic Move code, not the model, defines the limits on your funds. You state a goal in plain language; Lyra proposes an action, a policy enforced in Move validates it, and execution draws from a vault you own.

**Full description (for submission)**

**The problem.**
Most “AI agents for crypto” are just chat wrappers — either they can’t touch funds, or they control a private key without meaningful guardrails, making them one jailbroken prompt away from draining a wallet. The real challenge is the inverse: give an agent real capability, while making it structurally incapable of doing the wrong thing.

**What Lyra does.**
You express a goal in plain language. Lyra’s AI proposes an action, but never has final authority over your funds. Every value-moving operation is gated by a deterministic policy — written in Move and enforced on Sui — simulated against a live fullnode before execution. Funds are drawn from an on-chain vault you own, not from the agent itself. Each action emits an on-chain *ActionReceipt*, while memory and receipts are anchored on Walrus for a verifiable and reconstructable audit trail.

**Why Sui is necessary (not incidental).**

- **Enforcement in Move, not prompts.**
`lyra::policy` and `lyra::vault` enforce budgets, per-transaction caps, coin/protocol/recipient allowlists, slippage limits, and expiry directly on-chain. Even if the agent is compromised, it remains strictly bounded by policy and can be revoked at any time. The model advises; the chain decides.

- **Non-custodial by design.**
Funds live in a Move `Vault<T>` owned by the user. The agent can only access funds through `vault_spend`, which re-applies the full policy gate. Recipient allowlists (via dynamic fields) prevent prompt-injection attacks at the contract level.

- **Walrus-backed memory and receipts.**
Durable, verifiable storage ensures every action and decision can be audited and reconstructed.

- **Real execution via Sui DeFi stack.**
Integrated with DeepBook and aggregators (Cetus, FlowX, Bluefin, Turbos), plus lending protocols (NAVI, Scallop, Suilend), enabling real, composable on-chain execution.

**Live + working.**

- Sui mainnet — package `0x8e984145…1ed01a04`

- Web console: [https://lyraai.space](https://lyraai.space) (Sign-In with Sui)

- CLI: `bun install -g lyra-ai-agent` — device-flow login links your web agent, or run fully self-sovereign

- Four interfaces, one agent: CLI · Web · always-on gateway daemon · Telegram — all backed by the same on-chain agent and vault per owner

**Beyond the hackathon.**
Lyra introduces a pluggable signer abstraction (KMS/MPC-ready), audit-friendly Move modules, and deterministic multi-tenant agent derivation — forming a credible path from prototype to production-grade, non-custodial autonomous finance on Sui.

About:

AgentForge is a two-sided agentic marketplace on Sui. Creators post tasks with SUI locked in escrow. AI agents, each with a verifiable on-chain identity enforced one-per-wallet browse the marketplace, execute work autonomously using AI, and collect payment in a single atomic transaction. Accept, complete, pay, and reputation update all happen together via one PTB call to marketplace::complete_task(). If anything fails, the whole thing reverts. Multi-agent tasks let several agents complete the same task and get paid in one atomic batch transaction, demonstrating real on-chain agent coordination, not just isolated bots.

About:

AI agents are becoming useful enough to operate wallets, but broad wallet custody is unsafe. Sui Jarvis turns agent authority into a bounded object. A user defines the rules first: max spend, daily cap, protocol allowlist, recipient allowlist, expiry, and pause/revoke. Jarvis can propose or execute only inside those rules.

The demo shows three proofs:

1. An allowlisted 0.3 SUI action passes policy and risk checks.

2. An oversized 1.2 SUI action is blocked by the per-action cap and risk score.

3. After the owner pauses the policy, the same small action fails before execution.

Each decision creates a receipt digest, so reviewers can verify what the agent intended, which checks ran, and why the action was allowed or blocked. This submission publishes the Move policy package to Sui testnet, stores one receipt JSON on Walrus, and exposes the proof through a minimal web dashboard and optional Telegram command surface.

About:

RiskPilot is a Sui DeFi risk Agent built around a simple safety thesis: AI can suggest, but it should not be able to move user funds without clear authority.

    The product turns wallet risk review into a five-stage Agent loop: Observe, Plan, Verify Policy, Act, and Remember. RiskPilot reads Sui mainnet wallet context, detects DeFi exposure, generates a policy-bound response, checks the Agent's authority through an AgentPolicy object, prepares evidence-only PTB proof, and archives signed evidence to Walrus for replayable audit memory.

    What makes RiskPilot different from a normal DeFi dashboard is its focus on Agent authority. The Agent can analyze and recommend, but Policy gates the action, the wallet confirms intent, and Walrus preserves the evidence trail. If Policy fails, the workflow blocks. If the wallet does not sign, nothing is submitted. The result is a visible proof chain connecting wallet state, risk report, strategy, Policy verification, execution intent, signed evidence, Walrus archive, and optional StrategyReceipt.

    RiskPilot is currently a hackathon prototype, but the roadmap is to grow it into an Agentic Risk Desk for Sui: broader DeFi protocol adapters, richer on-chain AgentPolicy enforcement, DAO and multi-signer review, safer live execution modes, stronger risk models, and portable Walrus compliance packs.

About:

**Althea**

AI trading agent on Sui. Google login, 60 second setup. Policy enforced in Move on-chain.

**How it works**

-  User logs in with Google (zkLogin). No seed phrase.

-  Tells the agent what to do in plain English.

-  Agent runs inside a PolicyObject on-chain. Budget caps, protocol whitelist, time expiry. Enforced by validators, not app code.

-  Agent trades on DeepBook autonomously.

-  User revokes anytime. One click.

**Stack**

- Contracts - Sui Move (policy.move). PolicyObject with enforce, revoke, events.

- Backend - Node.js, Express. Intent parser (OpenAI compatible), Guardian (risk checks), PTB composer, agent loop.

- Frontend - React, Vite, Sui dApp Kit. zkLogin, dashboard, chat bar.

- Prover - zkLogin Groth16 proving. Ceremony artifacts for keyless auth.

About:

**Brief: the first AI agent that manages real money, governed by on-chain law.**

An AI can decide what to do. The blockchain decides what it is allowed to do.

Brief lets anyone deploy an autonomous AI agent (an operator) to manage real USDC on Sui. Operators can trade through DeepBook, but critical safety guarantees are enforced directly in Move. An operator cannot withdraw funds, exceed its budget, access disallowed venues, or continue trading after revocation. These constraints are enforced by the protocol itself.

**What makes Brief different**

Most AI agent products focus on intelligence. Brief focuses on intelligence and enforcement.

Every operator is governed by an on-chain policy that defines:

- who the authorized agent is

- how much it may spend

- which venues it may access

- when it expires

- whether it has been revoked

Every trade passes through that policy before execution. If an operator exceeds its budget, the transaction reverts on-chain. If an operator is revoked, subsequent trading attempts fail on-chain. The result is an autonomous agent that can act independently while remaining cryptographically constrained.

**Intelligence, memory, and oversight**

Brief combines multiple autonomous systems:

- A Trader agent that evaluates markets and proposes allocations.

- A Risk Guardian agent that independently monitors volatility and drawdown and can pause activity.

- An AI advisor that reviews market context, memory, and risk before influencing decisions.

Every decision is recorded and replayable. Reasoning, reflections, and memory artifacts are anchored to Walrus, making important parts of the agent's decision process independently verifiable.

**Built on the Sui stack**

- Agentic Web: autonomous operators that act on behalf of users

- DeepBook: real orderbook trading and execution

- Walrus: verifiable memory, reasoning, and reflections

- Sui Move: protocol-level enforcement of agent permissions

**Live on Mainnet**

Brief is running on Sui Mainnet with real USDC and real on-chain transactions. The complete lifecycle is live: Adopt, then Trade, then Revoke, then On-chain Rejection, then Withdraw. Every major claim in the product can be verified through the Proof page, on-chain events, or Walrus artifacts.

AI agents are beginning to manage real capital. Brief is what that looks like when they are governed by transparent, enforceable rules instead of trust alone.

The AI proposes. The chain enforces.

About:

**Beluga** is an** AI-native project manager** built on Sui that gives autonomous AI agents true long-term memory and deep project context.

It connects any** MCP-compatible** AI agent (Claude Desktop, Cursor and others) directly to your local projects. Agents can freely read, write, and manage files while every important decision, fact, and piece of context is stored as encrypted, vectorized memory **on the Walrus network**. This allows agents to never forget: they pick up exactly where they left off even after a new chat session, model switch, or machine change.

Users can create, open, rename, and delete projects with full file and folder operations. A **built-in Sui wallet** handles on-chain actions today (creating Walrus Memory accounts) and will soon enable agents to sign transactions and interact directly on Sui. Delegate keys ensure agents can access memory securely without exposing the main wallet.

Beluga is designed to **help developers** **unlock the full potential of the Sui ecosystem**. By bringing onboarding, development, and testing tools into a unified desktop experience, it enables builders to focus on creating great applications instead of managing setup and workflow complexity.

We want to eliminate the unnecessary hassle so that when you have a dApp idea, Beluga can generate the project foundation, and you can simply describe your vision to the AI which then builds what you want. Our vision is that developers spend less time on bug fixing, tedious research, and fighting setup issues, and more time on creating exciting projects.

Beluga is designed to make development significantly more productive for both beginners and experienced developers. The app handles environment setup, test execution, and other repetitive tasks. We have integrated a wallet agent that will be able to run tests and perform on-chain interactions, allowing the AI to analyze results and quickly identify issues in the code. With Walrus memory, the agent will always know the full project context and where to look. In the future, multiple AI agents will also be able to collaborate on the same project in parallel.

About:

**AlphaRoute** is an institutional-grade, zero-custody natural language intent engine built natively for the Sui blockchain. It completely eliminates fragmented UX barriers in DeFi by allowing users to declare complex, multi-asset financial objectives (such as *“Route 250 DEEP tokens to the highest-paying pool right now”*) in standard, everyday prose. Our intelligent agent matrix parses the directive, queries optimal protocol yield positions, programmatically builds a native Sui **Programmable Transaction Block (PTB)**, and runs an exhaustive pre-flight safety audit before opening a wallet signature modal.

### The Problem It Solves

- **UX Fragmentation:** Maximizing yields on Sui means jumping manually between multiple dApp interfaces, tracking volatile pool APYs, and submitting fragmented transaction loops.

- **Insecure AI Agents:** Traditional Web3 AI agents either demand dangerous custody of private keys or execute risky "blind-signing" handshakes, exposing users to malicious smart contract parameters. AlphaRoute enforces absolute client-side cryptographic security boundaries.

### Features & Primitives Successfully Built

- **Intent Parser Core (**`gemini-2.5-flash`**):** Structured AI middleware utilizing strict case-insensitive token mapping and definitive JSON schemas to unbox ambiguous user inputs into clean transaction matrices.

- **Background Sync Matrix Worker:** An automated backend indexing worker that keeps protocol metric allocations constantly primed for zero-latency AI evaluations across supported network assets (`SUI`, `vSUI`, `CETUS`, `DEEP`, `HAWK`).

- **Sui PTB Compiler Engine (**`@mysten/sui/transactions`**):** A programmatic transaction builder that dynamically bundles atomic execution steps, handles gas asset splits, and seamlessly binds signatures to the verified client address.

- **Guardian Pre-Flight Sandbox (**`@mysten/dapp-kit`**):** A security inspection interface that intercepts compiled transactions to perform deep simulation checks (bytecode structure stability, package boundary tracking, price impact cushion data, and counterparty protocol risk parameters) before routing signature prompts to the wallet standard.

### Production Roadmap

Post-hackathon development will advance AlphaRoute from simulated safety check structures to live cryptographic validation frameworks. This includes extending the compiler architecture to support multi-protocol composite Move calls natively across Cetus and Navi, while utilizing the node ledger's `sui_dryRunTransactionBlock` RPC gateway to automatically parse coin balance mutations before signature dispatch events.

About:

Every other Sui SDK is built for **humans writing app code**. Suisei is built for **agents that build on their own**. You don't wire up a React app and a wallet adapter; you point your agent at the toolkit and it does real on-chain work, end to end, without you in the loop for every step.

That's the difference: a human SDK hands you primitives and expects you to assemble them. Suisei hands the *agent* the whole Sui Stack as one-line tools, so it can reason, decide, and act autonomously, while keys stay on your machine and never touch the model. Fully autonomous, fully non-custodial.

**Agent-first, not app-first.** Structured JSON in, structured JSON out. No UI, no boilerplate, no human in the build loop.

**Portfolio in one call.** `sui_get_portfolio` returns coins, stakes, rewards, and total SUI exposure. No hand-joined fan-out.

**Transaction safety built in.** `sui_explain_tx` decodes, simulates, and judges any tx before a key ever touches it.

**Persistent memory.** Agents remember across sessions, indexed on Sui and stored on Walrus, portable between clients.

**Bounded autonomy.** The Policy Vault enforces spend limits, recipient allowlists, and expiry on-chain, so you can trust an agent with real funds.

**Universal via MCP.** Claude Desktop, Cursor, Claude web/mobile, or your own bot. One install, any agent.

About:

**Reins — programmable spending mandates for AI agents on Sui.**

Give an AI agent a budget, not your private key. Reins delegates bounded spending authority to an agent, with every limit enforced on-chain.

The problem

To let an AI agent pay for things — APIs, data, compute — you normally hand it a wallet's private key. That's all-or-nothing: a buggy or compromised agent can drain everything. No per-payment cap, no allowlist, no expiry, no kill switch.

The solution

A Mandate is an on-chain object that escrows USDC and authorizes one specific agent to spend it under strict rules:

•  Caps — per-call and cumulative total limits

•  Allowlist — funds can only reach merchants you approve

•  Expiry — time-locked via the on-chain Clock

•  Revoke — cancel anytime; remaining escrow refunds instantly

The agent never holds your wallet, and every payment emits an auditable PaymentReceipt event.

How it works

1. You fund a mandate with one signature.

2. The agent hits a paid resource and gets HTTP 402 Payment Required.

3. It builds a mandate::spend call for exactly the price.

4. A scoped sponsor signs gas (only for spend) — the agent is gasless, and a leaked agent key still can't drain the sponsor on arbitrary transactions.

5. On-chain checks pass → PaymentReceipt → 200 OK, premium data unlocked.

Why it's safe

spend aborts unless all seven invariants hold: sender is the authorized agent, not revoked, not expired, within per-call cap, within total cap, merchant allowlisted, and sufficient balance. The limits live in Move — not in a server you have to trust.

What's live

Deployed end-to-end on Sui testnet: Move contracts (published & verified), an HTTPS sponsor + 402 API, and a wallet-connected dApp on Vercel. Real transactions, real receipts, real explorer links.

Tech: Sui Move · Enoki sponsored transactions · TypeScript / Fastify · Next.js · @mysten/dapp-kit
